# Orbit prod release

## What this is

The goal is a production release with an empty ticket board. First, Orbit moves to its new stack (Batch M): Render for the API, web, landing and Postgres, Amazon SES for email, a separate staging, and production deployed only through release workflows. Everything else is parked until Batch M is done. Complete the redesign on web and Android, obtain the owner's whole-redesign approval, then complete the remaining batches and `/prod-readiness` findings. Use the granted canvas under `DESIGN.md` and keep web and mobile behavior aligned.

## Standing rules

- Finish the original spec. A blocker becomes the next task. An early stop needs an external cause: allowance exhaustion, machine stop, or the owner's stop request.
- Finish harness work before the redesign. Fix a harness defect in the current run; file a separate ticket only for a real capability gap.
- Report progress in product terms through `/progress`, with short replies that meet the writing contract.
- Run `/questions` before asking the owner anything. Check code, ticket comments, decisions and research first. Ask every surviving question in rounds of at most four.
- Decide questions with a clearly better answer. Follow `DESIGN.md`, `BRAND.md`, the granted canvas and brain decisions. Send genuine product, brand, price and design choices to the owner.
- Choose the complete, correct implementation. Speed changes scheduling, never quality.
- Habit create and edit begin with the required plain-language Task/Habit choice and keep an editable account-calendar date before More details. Tasks edit dueDate; dated habits edit their persisted startDate, independently of the next-due cursor. Habit retains fixed, count-based and distinct undated General schedules, and end dates can be cleared. Local parsing and the manual calendar remain usable offline and at capacity; wording the local parser cannot resolve goes to Astra automatically when online and within the allowance, and the resolved date carries a subtle Astra glyph until the person edits or picks a date (owner order on `#1714`, which replaced the explicit date pill and the Proposed candidate). Web keeps connection-required saving and Android keeps its existing offline saving. The API-first #1585 contract precedes publication of UI #1586; #227 is closed after its production read-back and Android Open 1.3.98 verification. Android production release needs a newer explicit order.
- Write and approve every piece of user-facing copy in the run, in both locales: write it against `BRAND.md`, the brain decisions and `/humanizer`, and approve it with `/second-opinion` before merge. Never hold copy for the owner and never add an owner copy-review gate. Pricing, positioning and brand direction stay the owner's (the rule above).
- Orbit has no ads; Orbit Pro is the only monetization. Ad code anywhere is dead code to delete, never a product option or a question.
- The Supabase project is deleted and production data lives on Render Postgres. The Batch E query-shape tickets remain as performance work on Render Postgres: measure bytes and rows per query shape, rank them, and fix the largest waste first; they are no longer a quota emergency.
- Batch M (the move to the new stack) outranks every other batch; everything else waits until staging and production both run on the new stack.
- Operate every platform through Terraform, CLIs, APIs and MCPs. A console step goes through `claude-in-chrome` only when no API covers it. The owner does only account creation, passwords, 2FA, payment and legal identity.
- A secret that only a browser can create (a first API token) is stored by the owner in the macOS Keychain; commands read it with `security find-generic-password -s <service> -w` and never print it. Keychain services in use: `orbit-cloudflare-api-token`, `orbit-stripe-test-secret-key`.
- After a migration is verified live, nothing stale stays active: cancel the retired subscriptions and delete the retired projects, after a final backup. A permanent deletion that only a browser can do is the owner's click.
- The production database copy loses zero rows: writes frozen during the dump, every table verified by row count and checksum, the old database paused until the owner signs off.
- Before the public launch, a production outage keeps its place in the batch order; it jumps the queue only after launch.
- Record every infrastructure decision and research result in the brain (`/brain`) as it is made.
- Nothing deploys on a merge or a push. Each code repository has one manually run `release.yml` with an environment choice: `production` always deploys `main`; `staging` deploys whatever branch the operator selects (for example `main` to test new features before production, or `redesign/main` for the redesign gate). No branch is tied to staging, and no staging service auto-deploys. The `/release` skill runs these workflows and takes the environment and, for staging, the branch.
- Release credentials are scoped by GitHub environment: each release job reads `RENDER_API_KEY` from its own environment (`production` or `staging`, plus `render-operations` for the API's scheduled Render jobs), and every environment that holds the key allows deployments only from `main`. No repository-level Render key remains once each repository's credential ticket lands.
- Never read a live secret into the session transcript. Pipe it from its source (an environment variable, the Keychain, SSM) straight into its destination, such as `gh secret set` on stdin. A secret that exists only in a dashboard and has no API is not used; choose a design that does not need it.
- Android releases keep a track choice with only the tracks in use: open and production build `main` against the production API; the internal testing track (the owner's closed beta) builds the selected branch against the staging API. Every other track option is removed. Never promote a binary between tracks, because the API base is baked in at build time.
- After a verified cutover, cancel the plans Orbit no longer uses (Vercel Pro, Resend Pro); permanent deletion of an old project or its data stays the owner's (Supabase and the two Vercel Orbit projects are deleted; Resend is left).
- Android fixes merged to `main` ship to the open track through `/android-release` (through `/release` once Batch M lands).
- Route all work to `main` in every repository: the redesign shipped by promotion, so nothing targets `redesign/main`.
- `redesign/main` is retired: `orbit-api` and `orbit-ui-mobile` archived it as an `archive/redesign-main-<date>` tag and deleted the branch; `orbit-landing-page` still holds a stale `redesign/main` that nothing targets.
- Do console and dashboard steps yourself through the `claude-in-chrome` skill (Play Console, AdMob, Cloudflare, Render, Supabase): a manual step is the run's work unless it needs the owner's password, 2FA, payment or a legal identity choice. A Play Data safety change waits until the build it describes is live.
- Use the configured local worker cap. Relaunch killed workers after checking their worktrees for commits.
- `/crank` sets `crank: true` in the run state and the run then schedules at maximum velocity (`.claude/skills/crank/SKILL.md` once `ui#2047` merges); a relay carries it, and a cranked handoff is `/handoff --sleep --crank` with a `## Crank` section, until the owner runs `/crank --disable`.
- Written authorization covers the requested scope. Ask again only if scope or inputs change.
- The centered phone layout is the large-screen answer. The public Play developer name is TL SOFTWARE ENGINEERING LTDA.
- Build device-dependent work without treating device inspection as an implementation gate. The owner tests the whole redesign from an APK at the redesign gate. Never boot the owner's Android emulator (`Orbit_Pixel_9_API_35`). A device bug may be reproduced on a separate throwaway AVD built from the installed arm64 play image, booted headless, with no personal account on it, and deleted once the fix is proven.
- Gate both listing and revoking API keys behind the emailed code.
- Render Astra responses with visual blocks wherever useful; simple sentences may stay text. Check `#318` before implementing the component plan. Use `beautifului.dev` when it supports the platform.
- Never repeat a paraphrased user report as a verified cause; check the original report.
- New reports affecting the shipped product take priority over the queue.
- Never edit `node_modules`. Confirm installed interfaces by reading them, and implement supported changes in repository code or config plugins.
- Route component-library migration after the redesign: rn-primitives on mobile and Radix on web, with shared component shape and Orbit tokens.
- The failed-delete toast pauses on hover and focus; Retry remains its sole action.
- Keep the Mac awake while a session runs. A closed lid still sleeps it.
- A sleep run does its visible Chrome work (width sweeps, Play Console wizards, Google account steps, bringing the automation window forward) whether or not the owner is using the Mac or talking to the session; sleep mode lasts until the owner turns it off.
- Chrome work, the run's own and every subagent's, happens in one tab of the Chrome window that is already open: never a new window, never a popup, never `window.open`, never `resize_window`; measure at the window's current width and record it. Page reads and browser steps go through the `claude-in-chrome` tools, never `orca computer` keystrokes (owner instruction); when this session has no tab group, create one, use its one tab and close it after. `orca computer get-app-state --restore-window` remains only to bring a hidden Chrome window forward. A subagent's click on an in-app delete can be refused by the permission classifier; the orchestrating session does those deletions itself.
- Buttons follow the drawings: every pill is `sm` except the single primary action that ends a flow (a form submit, the Wrapped player actions, the full-width action of a mobile overlay), and two or more pills acting on one section form one trailing action row of one size with a 12px gap; an action on one list item is that row's own action slot (`#1123` brings `DESIGN.md`, `PillButton`, an `ActionRow` primitive and the guards in line).
- Turnstile enforcement starts only after web and Android sign-in send a token.
- Keep locale out of auth and deep-link URLs.
- `/handoff` ends its session after committing the spec and one `NEXT.md`. `/wrap-up` runs `/progress`, `/questions`, then `/handoff`.
- The orchestrator merges a pull request with `gh pr merge --squash --match-head-commit <sha>` after the exact head has green checks, a Pullfrog approval submitted after its push, and zero unresolved threads. Every pull request merges to `main`. Production auto-deploy is off: a merge to `main` deploys nothing, and production deploys only through the release workflows.
- Admin merges happen only inside `/merge-prs` after the owner invokes it for an approved frozen set. Never use a direct merge API.
- Gate every Codex launch with the current explicit overrides `LOAD_MAX=60 SPACING=45`, holding the shared launch lock. Never pipe a launcher into `head`, because its final line dies on EPIPE.
- Start a waiter only as a background task. A trailing `&` does not wake the session.
- Check every colour, size and shape a worker adds against `DESIGN.md` before pushing. Read the worker's diff for gate edits before pushing (D95).
- After `merge-review-batch-body.mjs`, grep the body for superseded design claims that survived the merge.
- Link tickets in `orbit-api` and `orbit-landing-page` pull request bodies as `thomasluizon/orbit-tickets#N`; a bare `#N` points to that repository's issues.
- When a pull request body edit re-runs Guards, its concurrency group cancels the push's run. Read `gh run list --commit <sha>` before calling a check red.
- A pull request behind `redesign/main` may merge at its approved head after the merge result passes locally: both type checks, three Vitest suites and i18n usage for `orbit-ui-mobile` (`tools/check-i18n-usage.mjs` exists only on `redesign/main`); `dotnet build` and `dotnet test` for `orbit-api` (D115). Read test results by exit code, never by grepping summary text, because the unset locale prints the summary in Portuguese. When the base moved after the test, the result still counts if the files the base changed do not overlap the pull request's files.
- A Pullfrog approval counts only at the exact current head and only if submitted after that head's push. When a push that changes only generated or mechanical files dismisses an approval and Pullfrog's incremental review posts none, request a fresh one with `node tools/list-bot-threads.mjs --pr <n> --repo <key> --wait-seconds 900 --re-review`; never push an empty commit to force a review.
- Pullfrog and Codex share the OpenAI allowance. This effort runs its workers on Codex only: workers, independent reviews and Pullfrog stay on the Codex path. The coordinator runs in the engine the owner starts it in. When the authorized allowance and credits run out, stop and tell the owner; never switch to Claude, create another account or purchase access.
- Use the authorized Codex weekly allowance and credits; the banked reset is already applied. Codex Cloud stays off. Large tickets, semantic carries and a round after one failed round use `deep` (`xhigh`); after a second failed round use `max`, never `ultra`. Follow the actual installed launch interface and keep its launch lock, measured load and spacing gates.
- The redesign keeps every production capability. A view, tab, action, setting or shortcut leaves only when the owner explicitly removes it, with a citation; a missing one is a defect to restore with production's behaviour, never an intended gap.
- Feature flags are agent first: the owner asks in plain English and the agent changes the flag through the admin-only Orbit MCP tool `manage_feature_flags` (`/flag` once `#1420` lands), lists before and after, and reports the change. The owner never sets a flag by hand. A plain flag gates code inside one build through `useFlag`; a version flag (`redesign`) picks the build: the edge router on web, the update channel on Android, and that build calls API v2.
- Repository workers use the configured Codex `gpt-6.1-sol` tiers: high by default, medium for mechanical work, xhigh for deep and max for a second failed round. A Codex coordinator uses the user-level Codex default, `gpt-6.1-sol` at `xhigh`, as the owner ordered. This does not change the application Astra model. Pullfrog runs only its Codex step: the three `pullfrog.yml` workflows carry no Claude fallback step, so a failed Codex review fails `pullfrog-approval`.
- The Pullfrog console model stays "Custom (set in workflow)" in all three repositories. A model stored in the console overrides the workflow's `model` input (a stored pick resolves to `openai/gpt-6-sol` whatever the workflow says); with the console on Custom, each review logs `model: openai/gpt-6.1-sol` and `agent: opencode`. The console then shows the Smart tab with no provider, which is how it renders no stored model.
- Start every `tools/launch-worker.mjs`, `tools/wait-ci.mjs`, `tools/wait-release.mjs` and local check as a background task with the Bash `timeout` set to 7200000. The default background limit is 30 minutes, and reaching it kills the launcher and its worker mid-run, leaving unpushed commits or a half-finished cherry-pick.
- Gate each worker launch in the same background command that starts it: use `LOAD_MAX=60 SPACING=45` under the shared lock. Checking load in one call and launching in another is not a gated launch.
- When everything the redesign needs is done (every redesign ticket merged and closed, every service released to staging from `redesign/main`, the Orbit Staging internal build uploaded, and a full rendered sweep finding no broken behaviour), tell the owner plainly that the redesign is ready for his test: send a push notification through the `PushNotification` tool and make it the first line of the run's report. That is the redesign gate moment; the run stops there.
- Onboarding on the redesign ends with a step that shows the person their 7-day free Pro trial, and that step always shows, even when the person skips every earlier onboarding step. An account that is not on a trial (for example a free account that reset itself and runs onboarding again) sees an Orbit Pro paywall instead, built well and the way good apps present one, following `DESIGN.md` `## Special surfaces` (Paywall), the Pro drawing and `BRAND.md`. An account that already has Pro sees neither, and onboarding finishes normally.
- On a Codex capacity error, inspect saved worker outcomes and commits first. Retry only unfinished work through a verified available Codex model; preserve the current model-selection evidence and credit ceiling. Completed workers are never relaunched blindly, and capacity never moves a worker or a review to a Claude engine.
- Keep `CODEX_HOME` short enough for the macOS 104-byte socket limit; a longer path fails with `path must be shorter than SUN_LEN`.
- The beta fleet permits a simpler deploy order while the owner is the only user, including relaxing deploy-API-first. Keep the full code contract.
- `#74` owns existing copy. Never revisit the redesign gate's timing because of how many screens remain.

- Redesign fixes merge once the exact head has green checks, a Pullfrog approval of that head and zero unresolved threads, and then `redesign/main` is released to staging. No screenshot gate sits in front of a merge.
- Every rendered sweep scrolls each screen to its end at both widths and checks the last element clears the pinned chrome; bottom clearance is the shell's job, never a page's.
- Redesign quality is judged on the rendered app: sweep staging screen by screen, at desktop and phone width, against the `DESIGN.md` rules, the screen's drawing in `design/canvas/`, `BRAND.md` and the brain decisions, and file what is wrong. A code-only audit never counts as a sweep. Sweeps repeat after every batch of merged fixes until a full pass finds no broken behaviour. The redesign scope is frozen (owner decision): a sweep files only broken behaviour (a crash or error screen, lost or wrong data, an action that fails or cannot be reached, text that cannot be read) into Batch R; every other finding (polish such as an offset, a load shift or a spacing difference) is filed into Batch 2b, after the gate.
- A sweep may create, log and delete test habits and send Astra messages in the owner's staging account. Never in production.
- Owner overrides of the drawings: Calendário is one centred column (month grid on top, the selected day below it, the view selector folded into the month header, 24px card padding), and the Calendário drawing is amended to match; the Perfil Suporte row opens the support form, not Astra; Perfil is reorganised into grouped sub-menus chosen with `/second-opinion` (this supersedes the earlier inline-settings decision; the old duplicate Preferências, Avançado and Recursos de IA pages stay gone as separate copies); Sobre carries no internal naming note. The Astra composer shows only on Hoje.
- Owner decisions where the drawings leave a gap (brain ADR `Redesign review answers on phone search, Google Calendar import and the trial upgrade page.md`): at phone width, search opens from one icon-only ghost button at the end of the Hoje date row, and the destination title bars go; Google events stay importable as habits, and the import, review and calendar picker live in a sheet opened from Calendário, with no separate sync screen; an account on a Pro trial sees the Pro pitch on `/upgrade`, and the Assinatura dashboard is for paid Pro only.
- Every Astra write shows a preview block the person approves (approve, edit, reject) before anything changes, whatever the API's risk level for that capability; the internal risk level never renders to the person, and one write is reported once, in one block (brain ADR `Every Astra write shows a preview the person approves first.md`).
- The redesign renders the drawn Toast in the shell notice slot now; `#580` later moves it onto the headless layer. A floating control no drawing has (the web back-to-top button) is deleted, not restyled.- A deletion that removes data keeps its confirmation (`DESIGN.md` `### The core loop is never mediated`: confirmation follows reversibility, and anything that removes data carries one).
- Production web recovers a stale Server Action after a release (`#834`), and staging web does too since its carry merged.
- Every focusable control in the whole app draws exactly one focus indicator, following its own shape. A double ring anywhere (a field inside a ringed wrapper, a button inside a ringed group) is a defect, and a fix proves itself with a layout guard, not a class-name unit test.
- A fix that touches a reusable pattern (a focus rule, a toast, a sheet) is checked on every surface that uses the pattern before it merges, not only on the surface that was reported.
- The orchestrator may run the hermetic layout guard locally before pushing a pull request's guard (`npm run build -w apps/web`, then `npx playwright test --project=layout <spec>` in `apps/web` with the lockfile-pinned Chromium from `npx playwright install chromium`) to observe it red with the fix reverted and green with it. Workers never run it.
- The local merge-result check before merging a pull request that is behind `redesign/main` (rule D115 above) also runs the hermetic layout project whenever the combined change touches `apps/web`. Two pull requests that are each green alone can turn a layout spec red together (a spec from one relied on a client refetch the other removed), and the Vitest suites alone never see it.
- Approve copy with `/second-opinion` by framing the new strings as a claimed copy defect, so `DISAGREE` means approved; framing the copy itself as the claim inverts the verdict. Post the verdict on the pull request before merge.
- After every merge to `redesign/main`, an open pull request whose CI ran on the older base keeps that result: when the base gains a fix its checks need, merge `redesign/main` into the branch (the readiness loop's merge step, no rebase, no force) and push, so a new run sees the fixed base.
- A Pullfrog behaviour finding on code a carry brought from `main` is fixed on `main` first (the same code runs in production), then cherry-picked with `-x` into the carry, and the thread is answered with that fix. It is never closed by a ticket and never fixed only in the carry.
- A hermetic layout spec that needs a particular profile sets it with `setLayoutProfileSession` (`apps/web/e2e/layout/profile-session.ts`): the server preloads the profile from the session cookie, so a browser route on the profile endpoint does not reach the first render.
- A new value in a response enum is a breaking change for any client whose schema enum is strict: the client that accepts it ships first (web and an Android release), or the API sends it only to clients that declare support.
- Several approved pull requests that are behind `redesign/main` may merge on one combined local merge-result check: a scratch worktree at the base with every head merged, then `npm ci`, `npx turbo run type-check --force`, `node tools/check-i18n-usage.mjs`, `node tools/surface-manifest.mjs --check`, `node tools/check-sonar-paths.mjs`, the three Vitest suites, the web build and the hermetic layout project, all by exit code.
- The load average rule reads the one-minute figure: above 20 on 18 cores, no new worker starts.
- Hover and focus follow the control's own shape everywhere, on both platforms: a pressed or hovered row, tab, chip or button fills its whole hit area with the control's radius (never a squared or partial rectangle), and every field or code box draws one focus indicator (never a ring inside a ringed wrapper). This is the highest-priority redesign defect class; a fix covers every surface that uses the pattern and proves itself with a layout guard on web and a style test on Android.
- The web type tokens (`--fs-*`) equal the granted canvas scale (12 14 16 17 20 22 28 34 44 60); a size outside that set needs a drawing that sets it.
- The MCP OAuth authorize page served by the API is a redesign surface: it follows the Entrar and Verificacao drawings and the tokens, with the orbital mark.
- The redesign is finished only when every redesign ticket on the board is merged and closed, every service that has redesign work (web, API, landing, Android internal) is released to staging from `redesign/main`, and a full rendered sweep of staging at both widths finds no broken behaviour. Polish found after the freeze waits in Batch 2b.
- Orbit Staging's store listing icon is the redesigned mark (`design/brand/exports/play-icon-512.png`), because staging runs the redesign; production keeps its own until the redesign ships.
- Every sheet footer uses the drawn trailing pair: a ghost Cancel, then the filled action, hugging their labels at the trailing edge (`Sheet.d.ts`, `Orbit Perfil.dc.html`), on web and Android; the centred stacked full-width pair goes (`#996`).
- The redesign works on foldable and multi-screen phones: the Galaxy Z Fold cover ("passport") and inner screens, the Fold Ultra, the tri-fold and a folding phone class device. Every rendered sweep includes foldable widths (about 480, 840 and 1100 wide) besides desktop and phone.
- Staging and production install side by side: the internal track builds `org.useorbit.app.staging` ("Orbit Staging", its own Play Console app on internal testing with the same tester list as production), and open and production keep `org.useorbit.app`. The Firebase project config holding both packages is the `GOOGLE_SERVICES_JSON` secret of the `android-internal` GitHub environment only.
- Run only one local hermetic Playwright run at a time: two at once collide on the mock API port and one run never executes.
- The hermetic layout project runs with reduced motion, so every transition lasts 0.01ms and a computed style read in the same task as a focus or state change sees the starting value; assert such styles with a retrying check (`toHaveCSS`) or after two animation frames.
- A pull request that conflicts with its base runs no CI at all, so "settled with no failing checks" is not green until the rollup shows the workflow checks; merge the base forward first.
- A rerun of a `pull_request` workflow reuses the merge commit of its original trigger, so a red caused by an older base never clears on a rerun: settle it with the local merge-result check (D115), which counts when the pull request shares no file with the base change, or with a base merge.
- A hermetic layout case that measures a resting pointer state rests the pointer at a point `document.elementFromPoint` puts outside the measured element: the compact shell has no sidebar, so (0, 0) lies inside `main`.

- Configure an external service from its current documentation, never from memory, and pick the current, scalable option over a legacy one. A permission marked "Legacy" is never the answer.
- Onboarding shows once per install (before sign-in) and once per new account (after its first sign-in); onboarding finished signed out counts for the account it signs into; sign-out never shows onboarding (brain ADR `Onboarding shows once per install and once per new account, never on sign-out.md`).
- The second-account and Claude-migration tickets are superseded by the current Codex-only order. Reconcile their ticket disposition without creating an account, buying access or switching engines.
- A browser task the owner asked for includes its own save and publish clicks; stop only before an action the browser rules reserve for him (a password, a credential he must type, a payment, a permission only his account grants).
- The session chain ledger lives untracked at .git/orbit-session-chain.json.
- A long local check that must survive a context relay runs detached through `perl -MPOSIX -e 'POSIX::setsid(); exec @ARGV'` (screen refuses this account's login name), with a Bash background task polling its summary file for the wake.
- A carried `orbit-api` change whose Manual steps name staging Terraform gets that targeted apply from api `main`, for the named env groups, before the carrying staging API release. Production applies remain limited to the already-authorized named runbook targets; never apply untargeted Terraform.
- Base-merge a pull request that is behind its base only when it conflicts or its checks need a fix that landed on the base; a head that shares no file with the newer base merges at its approved head. Every base merge restarts about 180 job-minutes of `orbit-ui-mobile` CI and a fresh review.
- The confirmed GitHub Pro allowance permits 40 concurrent hosted jobs. Treat CI minutes per push as a shared capacity budget and avoid duplicate source pushes or unnecessary base carries.
- The redesign must feel like a native mobile app, not a desktop app squeezed onto a phone, on Android and on web at compact widths: show less on each screen and move secondary content and controls into sheets, menus, sub-menus, drawers, modals and toasts; give every group breathing room; give every hover, press and focus fill padding around its content; never let a label, tile caption or row title wrap to a second line or end in an ellipsis because the layout is too tight (shorten it, restructure it, or move it). `DESIGN.md` is wrong wherever it permits the opposite, and the owner's mobile review below is how it gets corrected. The owner's answers bind it (brain ADR `Native mobile review answers on suggestion chips, the Avisos bell and long typed text.md`): the Hoje suggestion chips live in the conversation and Hoje shows only the one-line composer; Progresso and Perfil carry a 48 row holding only the Avisos bell at the same trailing position as Hoje and Calendário, and it scrolls with the page; text a person typed or that comes from Google gets the full row width, then wraps to at most two lines, then ends in an ellipsis with the full text one tap away. At the default text size a product label fits one line; at a large accessibility text size (font scale above 1.3, web zoom above 130%, up to 200%) it may wrap inside a taller row and is never ellipsized or clipped. Row heights are minimum heights. A product label never uses the internal habit type names. Where a control sits on a screen is the run's decision for the end user, never an owner question.
- Astra opens full screen at every width, on web and Android, with its header, chips and composer inside it; the wide side panel goes. Web reaches Astra from an Astra row in the sidebar; Android keeps four tabs. The one-line shell composer exists only in the compact shell (web below 1024 and the Android app); on wide web the sidebar Astra row replaces it (brain ADR `Astra opens full screen and web reaches it from a sidebar row.md`, which amends D69's side panel and the no-Astra-entry rule).
- `/progress` reports the whole open session chain with equal weight: every relay entry's merges, releases and decisions, plus the current session, read from live git, release runs and tickets for the chain's full time window. A report that covers only the current session, or gives earlier relays a single line, under-reports.
- Within the redesign, harness tickets (the hermetic layout server, flaky layout cases, CI images, the tools harness) go first, then the remaining design tickets.
- A context relay closes the finishing session's Orca terminal once its successor is confirmed (through `orca-cli` first, computer use as the fallback), so an unattended night leaves one live terminal, not one per relay.
- The keep-awake hook (`~/.claude/hooks/keep-awake.mjs`) holds `caffeinate -dims`: without `-d` the display sleeps and the screen locks at once (the lock delay is immediate), which hides Chrome from every width sweep and Play Console step.
- Keep launchable work from the current order running at maximum safe Codex capacity. The measured load/spacing gates and actual resource constraints control scheduling; a running verification does not alone forbid independent work. A local resource guard is the next engineering task, never an invented ending.
- A command the owner types during an unattended run (`/questions`, `/progress`, a screenshot report) is answered before any other run work continues.
- During the owner's gate review, his findings are filed as Batch R tickets and fixed at once; the run never waits for him to finish testing.
- pt-BR copy reads as a Brazilian product writer would write it. A word-for-word translation of the en string is a defect anywhere in the app, the API's pushes and e-mails included; a pt-BR change is checked by a native-speaker review before it merges.
- A set of options never wraps onto a second line. Every no-wrap rule has a test that fails the pull request; a `DESIGN.md` line alone does not hold it.
- A progress report counts the redesign's open tickets (Batch R) separately from the whole board; the later batches never block the gate.
- The landing page is never a description on one side with a phone mockup, device frame or visual on the other. It follows the long-scroll reference `lp.pierre.finance/lp/converse` in Orbit's design, with Astra as the focus: a centred hero and device, product cards floating on scroll, Lenis smooth scrolling and GSAP motion, product compositions instead of illustrations, and no invented numbers or press. Compare renders side by side through independent Codex visual passes framed as claimed defects; no Claude judge or local Claude helper runs. This supersedes the one-screen `100dvh` landing.
- The staging landing posts to the staging API, and the staging API allows the staging landing origin; a landing probe that scrolls a control into view must respect the scroll-driven phone screens (`hittest-scroll.mjs`, not `hittest.mjs`).
- Nothing on the landing page changes content without a visitor action (scroll, tap, swipe or typing), so the landing carries no pause or play control and the phone screens never auto-advance. The one exception is the owner's order that the hero side cards float continuously the way Pierre's do (Pierre's own float and parallax script is the reference: three float paths, up to 17px, scroll parallax eased at 0.14), stopped under reduced motion and while the hero is off-screen or the tab is hidden; the phone itself does not float.
- Spacing is uniform across the whole app and the landing, and it includes interaction states: a hover, press or focus fill never touches or overlaps a neighbouring element (the habit detail title's hover fill covering the emoji tile and the frequency line is the defect class). A measured sweep of every screen of the app and the landing checks each interactive element's hover box against its neighbours at every sweep width, every finding is a Batch R ticket, and a layout guard on web and a style test on Android fail when a fill comes closer than the shared gap.
- Every component inside the landing's phone screen (pills, chips, the composer and its send control, cards, headers, thread spacing) is drawn at the app's own logical size and padding, scaled only by the one phone scale, with any larger hit area invisible; a probe compares each in-phone component's logical size with the app's value and fails on a difference. The phone screen never scrolls on its own: the wheel over the phone scrolls the page.
- Figma is dropped for the landing. Preserve rebuildable source, rendered evidence and the granted design/reference review; do not add a Figma upload, seat or API-limit gate before release.
- The owner checks the app again only once every redesign ticket is done, so the run drives every redesign ticket to merged and closed at full capacity, and fixes every CI red on an open pull request as it appears (owner order).
- A pull request event runs the workflows of the pull request's test merge commit, which GitHub recomputes lazily: after the base changes its workflows or required checks, a body edit or a reopen can still run the old workflows. Read `gh api repos/<owner>/<repo>/pulls/<n>` first (it recomputes `merge_commit_sha`), confirm the merge commit's parent is the current base, then edit the body to run `PR Metadata`, or close and reopen at the same head to run every workflow.
- A performance gate on a noisy metric (total blocking time) measures at least three runs and asserts the median; a single first run on a fresh CI runner measures the runner warming up, not the page. Diagnose a CI-only performance red with a bisect on the runner that saves traces, never with a local Lighthouse that cannot reproduce it.
- At the configured context threshold, launch nothing, drain, carry the evidence and follow the relay handoff procedure through a successor in the coordinator's engine. Preserve actual source/run ownership; a Codex coordinator never uses the Claude-spawning launcher or unmeasured Codex usage as if it were measured.
- The written redesign production approval is executed. Keep shipping approved live-defect fixes through the normal release workflows and continue the board; do not repeat the launch deadline or request another redesign approval.
- Orbit has no X brand account and gets none; the brand profiles are LinkedIn, Instagram `@useorbitapp`, Facebook, Discord and the Workspace mailbox. The founder account (one handle in the owner's real name on Instagram and TikTok, plus his personal X) is the marketing channel; the owner creates and posts to it by hand, and the run drafts its posts.
- The redesign is live in production, so a defect the owner reports in the redesigned app is a live production defect: it targets `main`, outranks the batch order and ships through the production releases.
- Android ships to the Open track, Orbit's main Android channel; the production track is cleared (`#1734`) and stays empty until the owner orders a launch; never upload to production without that order. Current carrying Open source, submission and unverified availability are recorded in Current state.
- Every `release.yml` takes a `sha` input: staging requires the full planned SHA (`-f sha=<40 hex>`), production defaults to the dispatch commit and refuses unless staging serves it healthy; `force_unstaged` is the recorded override.
- The staging Render Postgres resets at its free-plan expiry: the run recreates a free one and reseeds it; no paid plan.
- The context-relay successor runs in the coordinator's engine. `start-relay-successor.mjs` spawns Claude, which suits a Claude coordinator; a Codex coordinator needs a verified Codex-only successor first, and unmeasured Codex transcript metrics are unknown, not zero usage.

## The order

Reconcile the open board with these batches before each handoff. Place each new ticket exactly once, as one bullet in this section, and nowhere else in it. A batch completes before the next begins; a ticket that waits on a named condition does not hold its batch. Within a batch, use dependency order; drive already open pull requests first. The ticket bodies carry the verified scope, the facts and the folded content. The text of every batch before the board triage (closed work, owner rationale, round history) is `## Batch history` at the end of this spec: it is context, never placement.

Batches M (the move to the new stack), 0a, E (Supabase egress) and 1 (close the redesign) are done.

### Batch L: the owner's order, ahead of the rest

The owner's order: the landing page first, then every redesign ticket, then feature flags and API versioning. The one-minute load gate is the only launch throttle.

1. The landing page is done: the owner-approved page merged to landing `redesign/main`.
2. Every redesign ticket is merged and closed; the rest of the Astra plan is done.
3. Feature flags and API versioning: done. The redesign reached production by promotion to `main`; no routing Worker serves production, and `#1626` settles the `redesign` flag.

### Batch R: the owner's redesign review, and sweeps until nothing is wrong

The owner's review findings and the rendered sweeps (standing rules) land here. A sweep adds a ticket here only for broken behaviour; polish goes to Batch 2b. During the gate review, the owner's findings are filed here and their workers start at once.


Current live-defect and Astra work takes the stated priority before the rest of the board. The redesign is promoted, so carried and new shipped-product work bases on `main`; complete source, deployment and real staging acceptance before the full Astra sweep.

### Batch 0b: the harness and the standing syncs

These are the gates every later batch runs through. Re-read each ticket against the tree before building it, and never file a harness ticket as a substitute for a fix.

- `#1822` Accept current valid Astra tool arguments in the eval cases and derive the eval client's block kinds (harness drift found by the gate 6 failing-case rerun: `query_habits` now resolves today with `relative_period`, `describe_feature` `frequencies`, a hand-written `partKinds` list). Do not reopen the gate, repeat the freeze or stop current production-defect work for another redesign approval. Main already carries the promoted lint/suppression and scheduled contract-workflow changes; preserve the actual current protection rather than applying historical swap instructions.

### Batch 6: security, correctness and the deletions

A deletion ships as a deprecation, then a code removal, then a table drop, each in its own release. A DTO contraction waits on the `AppConfig.MinSupportedVersion` raise being live in the Play fleet. A deprecation's `[Obsolete]` raises SonarCloud `S1133`: Accept those issues with a comment naming the ticket's removal part, through the signed-in SonarCloud page.

- `#1654` Retire the last social column `Users.Handle`. Step 1 is live (`02b1a800`). Step 2a is live: `orbit-api#893` (`83dcd19f`) unmaps `Handle` with a no-op migration. Step 2b is live: `orbit-api#894` (`14508494`) drops `IX_Users_Handle_Lower` and the column on staging and production API, after a fresh Render export that holds the 147 legacy values; production still has 154 users. Next: step 3 removes the response key after the MinSupportedVersion raise (`#685`).
- `#685` Contract the API after the MinSupportedVersion raise: delete the colour-scheme write path and the ad reward profile field and user columns (folds `#765`), and remove the `redesign` flag seed row and the `features.redesign` config key (from `#1626`, closed: the flag is off with no accounts in both environments). Delivered as `orbit-api#923` (ad reward write path retired; the colour-scheme endpoint, request and onboarding parameter deprecated, because the pinned `oasdiff` check refuses a path removal without a deprecation; 14,507 tests pass, `oasdiff` exit 0). The flag seed and `features.redesign` stay, because the floor build `dfc321dc1` reads them (`apps/mobile/lib/providers.tsx:102,242`, `design-flag-refresh.ts:11`). The 86 lines `ConcurrencyRetryTests.cs` lost are the two `ClaimAdReward` retry tests and their helpers, which the ad reward retirement removes. SonarCloud's gate was D (two unused setters, one `CS0618` in a test, four deliberate `S1133`); round 1 (`12978116`, pushed) maps the retained ad reward dates through backing fields (no pending model change) and reads the obsolete request type by reflection. Round 2 (`7a3dc4a2`, pushed) accepts the capability inventory: the retained ad reward dates became read-only properties, so the inventory lists `LastAdRewardAt` and `LastAdRewardLocalDate` as removed Profile fields (the profile response is unchanged; 25 of 25 inventory tests). The four `S1133` issues are Accepted in SonarCloud with a comment naming this ticket's removal part; merged as `orbit-api#923` (`5cc4adccc`) and live on staging and production API (production run 40269364943 at `e0698655`, verified). Next: `#1654` step 3 after the floor raise. The code removal, the column drop and the flag removal follow in later releases.
