Merged as `orbit-api#892` (`1923b755`) and live on staging and production API; the targeted Terraform apply set `DesktopAuth__RedirectUri` in both API env groups. Staging acceptance passed 8 of 8 with a real sign-in (`desktop-1679/check.mjs`: other callback 400, no session 401, own callback 200 with `expiresIn` 60, wrong verifier 401, wrong callback 401, right verifier 200, replay 401); production was checked through config and unauthenticated 401s only, never by signing in to the owner's production account. Close when `#1560` step 2 merges and its desktop sign-in works.
- `#1560` Ship a real native Orbit desktop app for Windows and macOS: Tauri 2 loading the deployed web app (brain ADR D148, `The Orbit desktop app is a Tauri 2 shell around the deployed web app.md`), Electron measured as the runner-up; every web release updates it; no Mac App Store (owner): a notarized DMG and an unsigned Windows installer download from the landing page; no Windows certificate; the Apple Developer Program (shared with iOS) is bought at the last possible moment, so Mac notarization is the last step. The build plan is the ticket body. Steps 1 (`ui#2009`) and 2 (`ui#2015`) are merged and live. Steps 3 (`ui#2031`) and 5 (`ui#2037`, signed updates) are merged. The updater keys are provisioned: environments `desktop-staging` and `desktop-production` (production deploys from `main` only), one distinct Tauri key each, held in the macOS Keychain (`orbit-desktop-updater-<variant>-private-key`, `-password`, `-public-key`) and set as `TAURI_SIGNING_PRIVATE_KEY`, `TAURI_SIGNING_PRIVATE_KEY_PASSWORD` and the `TAURI_SIGNING_PUBLIC_KEY` variable. Desktop Release 0.1.1 for staging (run 40140756152, `main` `728b83ac`) succeeded: validate, the four platform jobs and publish all green; releases `desktop-staging-v0.1.1` and the `desktop-staging` update channel are published as pre-releases. The `desktop-staging` channel's `latest.json` names four packages and every signature verifies against the `desktop-staging` public key (posted on the ticket). Step 4's API half is `#1726` (below); the shell half (request the APNs device token on macOS and the WNS channel URI on Windows, keep the process running after its window closes, render the raw payload as one native notification, revoke on sign-out) follows once `#1726` merges. Windows stays truly unpackaged (owner: no Windows code-signing certificate; Microsoft's current push quickstart gives truly unpackaged apps foreground `PushReceived` delivery while the process runs and no COM background activation). Step 4 is merged as `ui#2067` (`ac4dfd93a`: APNs and WNS registration, the stable `deviceId`, the raw-payload notification and click routing, revoke before sign-out; the Windows C++/WinRT compile passed in CI) and live on staging and production web `e8ee19175`; Desktop 0.1.2 for staging (run 40207145219) is published and the `desktop-staging` channel names it for four packages. Production desktop is held until step 6 merges. Step 6 (release integration: `release.yml` and `/release` test the shell against the staging commit, publish a shell update when the shell changed, then release web; a web release reloads a running app safely; incompatible web changes are gated on the shell version) is `ui#2065` (worktree `ticket-1560-release-integration`, branch `feature/ticket-1560-release-integration`). Its main merge `c905e3517` (docs conflicts resolved; type-check, desktop 58, cargo 46 pass) was approved by Pullfrog but failed only `Authed Today Lighthouse budget`: script size 820752 against the 820000 cap, because both desktop bridges shipped in the browser bundle. Round 2 (`22f7abdc6`) lazy-loads the desktop bridges inside the shell and passed every check, the script budget included; Pullfrog then found that shell detection remounted the hydrated app and erased typed text, so round 3 (`64e469363`, max) loads the desktop controller beside the children and reports incompatibility back to the boundary (new SSR hydration test red before, 12 of 12 after; Today initial chunks 1,480,204 bytes). `64e469363` is approved with every check green (the iOS compile needed one rerun after a 45 minute timeout, `#1732`) and 0 threads, but it shares `apps/web/components/chat/answer-block.tsx` and `apps/web/__tests__/components/chat/choice-block.test.tsx` with `main`'s `ui#2057`, so it merged on the combined check (web and desktop suites exit 0) as `80fa8c43e`; the staging web release (run 40235938900) now builds and publishes the desktop shell before web. The history: it merged only after the combined check in worktree `combined-2065` (commit `e15777636`, tree of `main` `d2be6dd45` plus `64e469363`; `check-2065-combined.sh` writes `combined-2065/summary.txt`: npm ci and type-check passed, then the whole web suite and the desktop suite) passes, or after a base merge. A red there that also fails on plain `main` is `main`'s (see `#1732`). Then staging and production web, the first production Desktop Release, the desktop sign-in proof against staging, and close `#1679`. Rust is installed through Homebrew's keg-only rustup (`export PATH=/opt/homebrew/opt/rustup/bin:$PATH`).
- `#1726` Deliver Orbit reminders to the desktop app through APNs on macOS and WNS on Windows (the `orbit-api` half of `#1560` step 4). Merged as `orbit-api#922` (`b35f0175`) and live on staging and production API (runs 40192458538 and 40193001691, verified). Live acceptance waits on the owner's Apple Developer enrollment and an Azure app registration (free; account creation is the owner's); the senders stay inert while their values are absent.
- `#1563` Show the App Store badge and the Mac and Windows downloads on the landing page (after `#1562` and `#1560`).
- `#1777` Release the iPhone and iPad app automatically from `/release` and the release workflows, beside Android, with the same version (owner order; blocked by `#1561`).

## How the work runs

Use `/sleep` for unattended work and `/orchestrate` for one ticket at a time. Pullfrog reviews each pull request. Use the current head, latest review and required checks to decide readiness. `/handoff` updates this spec and overwrites `.claude/handoffs/NEXT.md`; it does not append a session chapter. Read `.claude/skills/orchestrate/SKILL.md`, `.claude/skills/handoff/SKILL.md` and `CLAUDE.md` for operating detail.

## Decision pointers

Open these brain notes BEFORE acting, through the Obsidian MCP (`mcp__obsidian__obsidian_list_notes`,
`mcp__obsidian__obsidian_get_note`, and `mcp__obsidian__obsidian_search_notes` when only the idea is
known); `cat` and `ls` miss the frontmatter and backlinks that record which decision superseded which.
They live under `2 Areas/20-29 Orbit Engineering/Decisions/`. A filename is a lead: list the directory
and copy the names that come back.

- `Feature flags live in the Orbit database and the agent changes them, and the redesign ships as a version flag.md` (D145, Batch L item 3; supersedes the PostHog flag part)
- `Spend the Codex weekly limit before the plan change and keep the credits for after.md` (D146, the Codex capacity plan)
- `Astra runs on gpt-6-luna at low effort with a per-user daily AI ceiling.md` (D147, the Astra model and its cost ceiling; supersedes the `gpt-6.1-sol` primary in `#582`)
- `Move Orbit to Render with Amazon SES, a free staging and manual release workflows.md` (Batch M; supersedes the next one)
- `Free accounts meet Orbit Pro only at a real boundary, never on a timer.md` (no recurring Pro prompt; the light hover value decision rides with it)
- `Redesign review answers on phone search, Google Calendar import and the trial upgrade page.md` (Batch R owner decisions)
- `Every Astra write shows a preview the person approves first.md` (Batch R owner decision on Astra writes)
- `Pro is Astra without the daily ceiling, and goals leave the paywall.md` and `Price Orbit at 9.99 USD and gate depth never the core loop.md` (what the onboarding trial step and paywall sell, and at what price)
- `Keep Supabase and fix the unbounded habit-list query rather than migrate.md` (the egress batch; superseded for hosting)
- [Historical redesign gate ADR](obsidian://open?file=2%20Areas%2F20-29%20Orbit%20Engineering%2FDecisions%2FThe%20redesign%20gate%20ships%20a%20closed%20internal%20build%20for%20%54homas%27s%20approval%20before%20any%20merge%20to%20main.md) (the current production approval supersedes its stop)
- `Run the rest of the redesign unattended and review it once as a whole.md`
- `Redesign PRs target redesign-main only until the redesign ships.md` and `The shipping branch outranks the redesign when the machine cannot run both.md` (branch routing)
- `An idempotency key for an AI tool retry binds to call position never to model-generated arguments.md`
- `An AI tool action reuses the app's own command never a raw repository call.md`
- `A shared timeless-text gate blocks a machine path an owner name and a dated anecdote across all three repos.md`
- `Gate new ticket work when shared CI and review capacity is full.md` and `An unattended run caps its own CI demand in code and waits on CI through a registered wake source.md`
- `A written instruction is the authorization ship on it without asking again.md` and [product-question routing ADR](obsidian://open?file=2%20Areas%2F20-29%20Orbit%20Engineering%2FDecisions%2FAsk%20%54homas%20product%20questions%20only%2C%20settle%20engineering%20calls%20without%20asking.md)
- `Reproduce a device bug on the exact shipped build before calling it fixed.md`
- `Onboarding shows once per install and once per new account, never on sign-out.md` (`#1084`)
- `Put rn-primitives and Radix Primitives under Orbit's own styling instead of adopting a universal UI kit.md` (Batch 4)
- `The Orbit workflow decision register (D1 to D42).md` for the older numbered decisions

Current operational rules above take precedence when a record conflicts.

## Constraints

- `apps/mobile/android/` is Expo prebuild output. Native fixes belong in app config or a config plugin.
- Mobile ICU syntax treats an apostrophe before `{`, `}` or `#` as a quote. Double it when the apostrophe must render. Test mobile strings through the app's i18n instance.
- Account deletion deactivates with a bounded grace period. Sign-in cancels deactivation; never claim deletion has no recovery.
- A streak freeze is banked automatically and spent by hand. `RepairableGapDates` does not depend on the freeze bank.
- `useCalendarEvents` is a bounded manual-import feed, not a complete events endpoint. The events API reports the event timezone.
- Retrospective access is controlled by the API setting; clients read the server answer rather than hard-coding Pro access.
- A Server Action strips thrown errors to `digest`; return serializable `ActionResult` values across that boundary.
- An Android accessibility node with zero opacity or fully off-screen is ignored. Use `AccessibilityInfo.announceForAccessibility` for announcements.
- Expo SAF copy deletes an existing destination document. Copy into the directory with the correctly named source.
- Android's key map emits arrow keys but no Home or End key names.
- `Harness Calibration` hashes complete normalized file content. Reseed after changing calibrated files and reconsider the verdict.
- `local/*` lint rules run at `error` with zero violations. Do not restore a suppression baseline or weaken a rule. Sweep callers before changing a shared primitive.
- A closed allowlist only shrinks. When a fix removes suppressions, lower `orbit-api` `tools/suppression-allowlist.json` to the observed count.
- Put parity mirror hook pairs in `sonar.cpd.exclusions`. Shared logic belongs in a React-free core in `packages/shared`; each app keeps only its React wiring.
- SonarCloud's API refuses pull requests targeting `redesign/main`. Read its gate from the check run's `output.summary`.
- Run the `orbit-api` suite with `LANG` unset and with `LC_ALL=en_US.UTF-8` to catch host-culture formatting.
- Run both harness suites after `.claude/**` changes: `node tools/test-tools.mjs` and `node .claude/hooks/test-hooks.mjs`.
- Pullfrog readiness depends on the last review of the exact head and the newest `pullfrog-approval` check. Review body findings count even when unresolved thread count is zero.
- A `parity:exempt` label does not change a run created before the label. Trigger a fresh pull request event after applying it.
- Regenerate the surface manifest when a drawn surface changes; record deleted IDs with their decision in `tools/redesign-groups.json`.
- Use `npx turbo run type-check --force` for evidence when cache freshness matters. Run type checks from the repository root.
- Installed dependencies are read-only evidence. Check their integrity with `node tools/check-dependency-edits.mjs` before citing them.
- `orbit-api` no longer commits `architecture.json` or `architecture.html`; run `node tools/arch-map.mjs` there before reading the map. The `drift` job only proves the generator runs.
- Workers often forget the `parity:exempt` label on a one-sided mobile change; Cross-Platform Parity then fails until the label and a `## Parity` line are added (`#736` moves the check into delivery).
- A worker branch has a launch cap of two; a review-fix relaunch beyond it needs `--relaunch-reason`, or the launcher refuses without starting a worker.
- Admission refuses new ticket work when open pull requests plus live reservations exceed ten across the three repositories; review fixes on open pull requests are still admitted.
- Every deploy goes through the manual `release.yml` of its repository (`production` from `main`, `staging` from a selected branch, always dispatched with `--ref main`) and `android-release.yml` (open and production from `main`, internal from a selected branch), run by `/release`. Render auto-deploy is off for every service.
- Render provider v1.9.1 turns a digest image path into a tag on any web service update, so an existing web service is never updated through Terraform; the release workflows own web digests (`ignore_changes` on the digest) and the `#814` guard rejects in-place web service updates before apply.
- Staging services: `orbit-api-staging`, `orbit-web-staging` (`srv-dass1t0jo6nc73d5s340`) and `orbit-landing-staging` deploy only through `release.yml` with a selected branch; today they run `redesign/main`.
- Staging billing runs on Stripe test mode: product, four prices and the webhook exist, with ids and secrets in SSM `/orbit/staging/api/Stripe__*`; `Stripe:PublishableKey` was deleted because nothing read it.
- A `gh issue list --search` call spends the separate search rate limit and can fail while the GraphQL budget is fine; probe `rateLimit` before concluding GraphQL is exhausted, and filter REST issue listings locally instead.
- `useorbit.org` DNS is served by Cloudflare (zone `3f80ecc2735314886702b6643b15d150`, nameservers `candy` and `tom`). Cloudflare DNSSEC signs the zone (KSK key tag 2371, algorithm 13); its DS record (digest type 2, digest `550CC9A0902AC2319B30F903A53F7D487E2172A4B2E65C587348A0867EC2DC46`) is added at Spaceship > useorbit.org > DNSSEC. Never add a DS whose zone is not the delegated, signed one: that breaks resolution for validating resolvers.
- Apply Terraform only with `-target` lists that exclude the web services and the production API service unless the change is intended; a full plan still carries items that need a release first.
- The repositories stay public: GitHub-hosted CI is free only for public repositories (one day measured 21,300 Linux minutes across the three), and CodeQL's licence covers only open source code. Never commit Terraform state or a secret.
- A sleeping staging API takes about a minute to wake, and requests during the wake return 429, which the web shows as "Ocorreu um erro" with the "Foram muitas tentativas" toast; a reload after the wake loads normally. The pinger keeps it awake from 08:00 to 24:00 Sao Paulo time.
- Render free web services sleep after 15 minutes without inbound traffic; free Postgres expires 30 days after creation, has no backups and is limited to one per workspace. Staging is built around both limits.
- The Render MCP creates services, triggers deploys, edits environment variables and reads logs, metrics and read-only SQL; it cannot delete or change other settings, so those go through Terraform or the Render API.
- Google Play numbers versionCode once per package across every track, so staging and production builds share one sequence. A binary promoted between tracks keeps the API base it was built with.
- The worker launcher reads `.claude/orchestrator.json` from the orchestrating checkout, which runs on `redesign/main`. A launcher change merged on `main` reaches workers only after the `#556` sync carries it. Codex workers start with `--disable apps --ignore-user-config` once it does, which also skips the user-level Codex hooks in `$CODEX_HOME/hooks.json`.
- A worker's final report does not always use the `## Test evidence`, `## Assumptions`, `## Manual steps` and one-lane-per-line `## Review harness` shape that `merge-review-batch-body.mjs` reads, and its log prints the final message twice. Rebuild the report in that shape from the last copy before merging it into a pull request body.
- Count Codex hook events as `hook: <Event>` lines (`LC_ALL=C grep -a`); the bare word "hook" also matches source text in the log.
- Play Console validates every active track, so an old build on the internal track blocks a new declaration or upload on another track. Replace the internal build with the current one first.
- The `## Review harness` block has one line per lane. A pull request with no animation change writes the motion lane exactly as `not applicable: no changed animation`.
- Start `tools/launch-worker.mjs` and `tools/wait-ci.mjs` as background tasks with no pipe and no `&`; a pipe ends the process early and the wake notification never arrives.
- The `orbit-api` `Mutation (domain)` job can reach its 45-minute timeout on a large diff. It is not a required check; rerun it once and record the timeout on the pull request.
- A React inline style that sets a shorthand and then the same property's longhand to `undefined` loses half the shorthand: React writes the `undefined` key as an empty string on the client, while server HTML omits it, so only client-rendered elements break. Set both longhands explicitly.
- Web and Android mirror files (the icon map, the icon registries, a screen's options menu) read as duplication to Sonar; list each mirror pair in `sonar.cpd.exclusions`. Sonar's API refuses pull requests on `redesign/main`, so infer the duplicated files from the diff.
- The Orbit MCP `bulk_log_habits` tool takes no idempotency key, so a replay check against production cannot go through it; `#390` needs a device test.
- Keep decision logs with times in the scratchpad, outside the repository.
- Render's Hobby workspace allows exactly two environments per project (Production and Staging exist). A service created before its environment group is linked starts without that configuration; redeploy it after the link. Render's trigger-deploy API may answer 202 with no body.
- Terraform for Render lives in `orbit-api` `infra/`, with state in S3 bucket `orbit-terraform-state-713285551626` (us-east-2) and secrets read from SSM `/orbit/<environment>/<service>/<KEY>`. The imported production API ignores its own env vars (they override the linked `orbit-production-api` group until removed). The Render project's environments are keyed by their live names; a different key plans a destroy of the Production environment. An empty `custom_domains` must be null.
- Plan and apply Terraform from the `orbit-api` checkout with `RENDER_API_KEY=$RENDER_MCP_TOKEN`, `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN` from the Keychain, the default AWS profile (`orbit-operator`), and an ignored `infra/local.tfvars` holding the web image digests; apply the web services only once their image exists.
- Vercel is retired and both Orbit projects there are deleted. The personal portfolio project there is not Orbit's and stays untouched.
- Dash Ban checks pull request titles and bodies too; strip em dashes from any worker report carried into a body.
- A worker order for `orbit-api` cannot file tickets itself (the ticket tools live in this repository); the orchestrator files what a worker reports. Tracked specs, prompts and skill output carry rules and current state without session history, dates, attribution or machine paths.
- The permission classifier blocks reading a live credential into the transcript unconditionally (`hard_deny`, Credential Materialization); no setting, permission mode or approval lifts it (https://code.claude.com/docs/en/auto-mode-config.md).
- Neither the Render API nor the Render Terraform provider exposes a service's deploy hook URL.
- AWS SES production access is granted (`sesv2 get-account` reports `ProductionAccessEnabled` true, 50,000 sends a day, us-east-2). CloudWatch publishes only account-level `AWS/SES` metrics, so attribute a send to an environment by timing one isolated simulator send.
- A leftover local `next-server` on port 3000 serves a stale build to a local smoke run. Check `lsof -nP -iTCP:3000 -sTCP:LISTEN` before starting one.
- Before a staging database replacement, the reseed workflow opens the Render Postgres allow list to the runner's own address only, restores the operator list after each step, and a separate reconciliation workflow repairs a run that timed out.

- Worker logs live in the OS temp directory (`orbit-workers` under `os.tmpdir()`), which a reboot empties; a worker killed by a reboot leaves commits but no report, so relaunch it with a continuation order naming its commits rather than writing its report by hand.
- An order composed before the main checkout fast-forwards lacks newer composer text (the Chromium Vitest carve-out arrived that way); recompose an order after every fast-forward before launching it.
- A Pullfrog raw model specifier passes through `resolveModel` unchanged, gets no effort rung from `resolveRunEffort`, and on the opencode harness sends no variant; `deriveSubagentModels` gives it no override, so `reviewfrog` inherits the main model. The Codex CLI harness (`PULLFROG_AGENT=codex`) disables `multi_agent`.
- `GitGuardian Security Checks` is a required context on `main` in `orbit-api` and `orbit-landing-page`; its check suite can sit queued for a long time in the vendor's backlog, and nothing but waiting clears it.
- Render's Hobby workspace keeps no HTTP request logs, so an empty request-log query proves nothing about whether a request arrived; use app logs, the browser's network panel or the API's own logging.
- The canvas drawings cannot render from a checkout (`support.js` and the design-system bundle are not committed), so compare against the drawing's markup, data block and report text, and against the design-system contracts in `design/canvas/_ds/<uuid>/components/*.d.ts`.
- A GitHub `Closes thomasluizon/orbit-tickets#N` line in an `orbit-api` or `orbit-landing-page` pull request closes the ticket on merge to `main`; use `Refs` when the ticket still has operations after the merge.
- A body edit on a pull request re-runs Guards, and the Review harness motion line must read exactly `not applicable: no changed animation`.

- The sweep's Chrome window usually reports `document.visibilityState` `hidden` (it sits behind other windows). In a hidden document requestAnimationFrame and CSS animations stall, so route transitions leave two layers on screen, a Base UI sheet can fail to move focus or to close on Escape, and a click can look dropped. Before filing any of those, run `document.getAnimations().forEach((a) => a.finish())` and re-check, or confirm in a visible window; static layout, text and computed styles are reliable either way.
- A web load at the wide width server-renders the compact shell and swaps to the wide shell after hydration (`#900`); screenshot a desktop page only after the sidebar shows.
- claude-in-chrome can be connected to more than one browser and may drive a remote one: run `list_connected_browsers` first and select the macOS browser local to this computer. That Chrome's smallest viewport is about 600px wide, which already renders the phone layout (web switches to the wide shell at 1024px); a window that will not resize and an `outerWidth` of 0 mean the session is driving the wrong browser. Read `innerWidth` and `innerHeight` after every resize, because a docked panel or a full-screen window changes the page viewport without changing `outerWidth`; a macOS full-screen window may refuse a narrower size, so leave full screen only through the window, never through a system setting. Staging refuses framing, so a same-origin iframe cannot emulate a width. A resize beyond the screen (1352 by 849 available) leaves the window hidden and frozen at its old size; stay within it (desktop 1352 by 820, phone 600 by 820). Closing the MCP group's last tab and calling `tabs_context_mcp` with `createIfEmpty` opens a fresh window at the last requested size, which is the way out of a frozen or full-screen window. The staging session cookie is host-scoped: the owner's Chrome holds a session on `app-staging.useorbit.org`. The extension may not act on `accounts.google.com`, and the run never reads a one-time code, so a lost staging session needs the owner's one sign-in.
- The hermetic layout harness serves an English profile (`test-support/hermetic/mock-api/fixtures/profile.ts`, `language: 'en'`): a layout spec reads English messages, and a `pt-BR` locale cookie makes the app reload mid-test. A spec that reads computed focus styles finishes running animations first (`document.getAnimations().forEach((a) => a.finish())`), because outline and shadow transitions start from `medium` and `currentColor` and a read right after focus sees values that never settle on screen. The harness returns no `/api/events/ticket`, so the server logs an `INVALID_RESPONSE_SCHEMA` there on every page; it is not a failure.
- Four web Vitest files launch headless Chromium for geometry (`apps/web/__tests__/support/chromium.ts` and its users); a worker order names that carve-out and requires the full Vitest run (`tools/compose-prompt.mjs`, `.claude/hooks/_lib/rules-worker.mjs`), so check the web file count in its evidence.
- The GitHub GraphQL budget is 5,000 points an hour, shared by every tool, worker and agent on the account. A loop over many tickets or worktrees (board repairs, worktree teardown) can spend it in minutes and stall every merge and review read until the reset; run such loops with a pause between calls and stop them on the first rate-limit error. `create-ticket.mjs`, `complete-ticket.mjs` and the board status helpers scan the whole project on each call and cost far more than a plain read, so file tickets in small groups and never loop `--repair-status` over a list; a `gh project item-list` of the whole board is expensive and its status column did not match the live status. `list-bot-threads.mjs` waits up to 900 seconds for a fresh review by default: pass `--wait-seconds 0` for a one-time read. Workers also spend this budget. REST endpoints (`gh api repos/...`) keep working while GraphQL is exhausted, and the REST `rate_limit` view can show a stale GraphQL figure: probe with `gh api graphql -f query='query{rateLimit{remaining resetAt}}'`.
- The ticket board's built-in "Item closed" workflow is off, so a ticket that GitHub closes from a merged pull request keeps its board Status and `teardown-worktree.mjs` refuses its worktree. Run `node tools/complete-ticket.mjs --issue <#N> --repair-status` on such a ticket; turning the workflow on would flip a `--cancel` close from Canceled to Done.
- The `orbit-staging-reseed` AWS role trusts the OIDC subject `repo:thomasluizon/orbit-api:environment:render-operations`; a job that assumes it must run in that environment.
- The Play Console users and permissions page does not persist changes made from a hidden automation window (the page renders with `document.visibilityState` hidden and froze once); grant Play permissions from a visible window, or ask the owner.
- On `orbit-api` `main` the `CodeQL` results check is not a required context (the `Analyze` jobs are); a verified false-positive alert is dismissed with a written reason (under 280 characters) and the CodeQL workflow rerun, as earlier `cs/user-controlled-bypass` alerts were.
- SonarCloud runs on every pull request, and its gate (new-code coverage at least 80 percent, reliability A) is part of green checks. Its API refuses pull requests on `redesign/main`, so read the gate from the check run's `output.summary` and compute coverage locally from lcov against the diff. Rule `css:S4662` flags any at-rule missing from its ignore list (`value,tailwind,screen,responsive,variants,apply,theme,source,utility,variant,custom-variant,reference,config,plugin,define-mixin,mixin`), so `@slot` inside a `@custom-variant` body is a MAJOR bug there; a Tailwind plugin (`@plugin`) carries such a variant instead.
- A worker order that carries the UI review sweep can loop its completeness critic past the 45-minute ceiling. A relaunch says: finish and test the started edits, run each lane once, list new out-of-scope findings as follow-ups.
- The hermetic mock API answers `{}` for any route it does not map (`apps/web/test-support/hermetic/mock-api/server.ts`), so a layout spec that visits Calendário routes `/api/habits/calendar-month` itself with a schema-parsed fixture, as `press-shape.spec.ts` does.
- Both shells render before hydration, so a layout locator for the tab bar uses `[data-shell-tab-bar]` and one for the sidebar `[data-shell-sidebar]`; a box read for a geometry assertion waits until it holds still (`readHitBoxOnceStill` in `press-shape.spec.ts`).
- claude-in-chrome sometimes reports a navigation that left the tab on its new-tab page; repeat the navigate call before reading the page.
- The staging and production Render web services have auto-deploy off (set through the Render API; Terraform's image runtime block has no auto-deploy field).
- Device repro recipe for Android (a throwaway AVD, never the owner's): build a universal APK from the release run's `android-release-aab` artifact with `bundletool build-apks --mode=universal` and a local key, install it, and approve the App Link for that signature with `adb shell pm set-app-links --package org.useorbit.app STATE_APPROVED app.useorbit.org` (the Play app signing SHA-256 `4C:00:41:...:F2:BB` matches the live `assetlinks.json`). Skip Chrome's first run without accepting anything by writing `_ --disable-fre --no-default-browser-check --no-first-run` to `/data/local/tmp/chrome-command-line` and running `adb shell am set-debug-app --persistent com.android.chrome`. To deliver a Google redirect without a password, read the `state` from the Custom Tab over `adb forward tcp:9333 localabstract:chrome_devtools_remote`, inject a full-width link to `https://app.useorbit.org/auth-callback?state=<state>&code=<fake>` through the DevTools protocol and tap it; Chrome hands it to the app as a `VIEW`/`BROWSABLE` intent, and a fake code shows up in production logs as one `Could not exchange Google sign-in code`.
- An instrumented local release build (temporary `[DEBUG-xxxx]` `console.warn` lines reach logcat as `ReactNativeJS`) needs, in a scratch worktree only: `node scripts/set-app-version.js <version> <code>` first (the committed `app.json` version is below `MinSupportedVersion`, so the build otherwise opens on Update required), a placeholder `google-services.json` for `org.useorbit.app` (push is inert in that build), and the release environment values (`EXPO_PUBLIC_API_BASE`, `EXPO_PUBLIC_GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID`, `EXPO_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_KEY`, `EXPO_PUBLIC_SENTRY_DSN`), because the PostHog gate's config fetch is part of the startup behaviour.
- Android Auto Backup restores only SharedPreferences minus `SecureStore` (the `expo-secure-store` backup rules include only the `sharedpref` domain), so AsyncStorage state such as the onboarding flag is never restored on a reinstall.
- `GET /api/config` is `[Authorize]`: a signed-out client gets 401 and falls back to `DEFAULT_CONFIG` (web always did; Android does after `#1010`).
- The orchestrator guardrail hook refuses a shell redirect whose target contains an expansion (`> $DIR/file`, `> $(...)`), so a scripted redirect names a literal path or runs from a helper script in the scratchpad.
- In the sweep's hidden Chrome tab, page timers are throttled hard: an evaluated `await new Promise(r => setTimeout(r, 3000))` can outlast the tool's 45-second CDP limit. Wait with the browser tool's own wait action, then evaluate without timers.
- A hermetic layout spec that opens Calendário on a Pro or trial profile also routes `/api/calendar/events` (and `calendar-month`): the harness answers `{}`, and the unvalidated events response crashes the page into its load error until `#1017` lands.
- A cold load of `/calendar?mode=review` opens the review sheet on staging once the data arrives; a check a few seconds after load can run before it and look like a failure.
- Render does not redeploy a linked service when Terraform changes its environment group; redeploy the unchanged commit (Render MCP `trigger_deploy`) so the service reads the new value, and confirm `/health` after.
- A reboot empties the system temporary directory that holds session scratchpads as well as `orbit-workers`, so a session scratchpad and every scratch merge-check worktree under it vanish; leave registrations to `git worktree prune`.
- In this shell `ls` is aliased to eza, which rejects `-t` with a path; scripts use `command ls`. A pipe whose producer fails can leave `awk` reading standard input and hang the call.
- `tools/create-worktree.mjs` called several times back to back in one shell loop fails with "orca worktree create failed with exit 1"; create each worktree in its own call.
- The Cloudflare account token in the Keychain entry `orbit-cloudflare-api-token` can list Workers scripts but not write them (`PUT .../workers/scripts/...` returns 403); adding Workers Scripts Edit to it is the owner's click.
- From the automation Chrome window, which reports `document.visibilityState` hidden, Play Console saved the Monetization setup but refused to save a new subscription base plan (only "Não foi possível salvar as alterações"); create base plans from a visible window.
- The production app's internal track still holds 1.3.39 (98), the old redesign gate build against the staging API; a tester gets the highest versionCode across tracks, so open builds win.
- Pullfrog runs as `workflow_dispatch` runs of `pullfrog.yml` on `main`, several at once; a pushed base-merge head can wait in that queue or get no review at all, which `--re-review` fixes (standing rule above).
- On Linux headless Chrome (the CI runners), CDP `Input.dispatchTouchEvent` and `Input.synthesizeTapGesture` produce pointer and touch events but no gesture events, so `:active` never applies to a held touch; macOS produces them. A Chromium test that needs a held touch uses DevTools touch emulation: `Emulation.setEmitTouchEventsForMouse({ enabled: true, configuration: 'mobile' })`, then a raw `Input.dispatchMouseEvent` that is not awaited (an awaited press never acks).
- A test file that switches a `vi.mock` between real and fake hooks on a module-level flag breaks any tree an earlier test left mounted: decide real or fake once per component instance and unmount every tree a test creates.
- `tools/wait-ci.mjs` ends the whole waiter with `HEAD_MOVED` when any of its pull requests gets a new push, and a transient GitHub read can end it with `READ_ERROR`: restart it, folding pull requests so no more than three waiters run.
- A `wait-ci.mjs --require-check pullfrog-approval` waiter that ends with `CEILING` while the check is unpublished means Pullfrog never reviewed that head: read the review with `--wait-seconds 0 --no-request`, then post a plain `@pullfrog review` comment if none exists.
- A worker log repeats the order's `NEEDS_DECISION: <one question ...>` template text; only a final `NEEDS_DECISION:` line that names a concrete question is a decision request.
- The Play Store shows an internal-testing app whose console setup is unfinished under a temporary name ("<package> (unreviewed)") with the default icon until the setup is complete and reviewed.
- Hermes on Android ships no `Intl.ListFormat` (and other newer `Intl` constructors), while Vitest runs on Node with full ICU, so shared code that constructs one passes every unit test and throws on the device. Mobile loads polyfills from `apps/mobile/index.js` before `expo-router/entry` (`#1076`). The pinned engine (`hermes-android` `250829098.0.17`, from `node_modules/react-native/sdks/hermes-engine/version.properties`) ships only `Collator`, `DateTimeFormat` and `NumberFormat` (strings in its `libhermesvm.so`, classes in its `classes.jar`), so `Intl.PluralRules` is missing too and `intl-messageformat` throws on every ICU plural (`#1085`).
- Orbit Staging is its own Play app with its own Play app signing key, SHA-256 `62:AC:19:77:F5:55:4E:89:4B:09:C3:21:F6:E4:0B:C8:27:77:37:3F:0A:F8:FE:A1:F1:23:CE:67:D2:FD:59:AA`; the upload key `51:C9:EC:...:D8:3F` is shared by both apps and never appears on devices. Every App Link host the staging manifest declares (`app-staging.useorbit.org`) needs that statement in its `assetlinks.json`.
- `tools/create-worktree.mjs` takes a numeric `--issue` (no `#`), and orca names the new branch `thomasluizon/<name>`: switch to `fix/<name>` and delete the orca branch before composing the order.
- A worktree without `node_modules` fails the pre-commit `i18n-usage` hook; run `npm ci` there (a symlink to another checkout's `node_modules` is refused by the hooks).
- In the hidden automation Chrome window, Play Console wizard sections (data safety categories) do not expand on click or script, and an evaluated `setTimeout` can freeze the call; `open -a "Google Chrome"` does not make the window visible. Finish those forms in a visible pass.
- The `/questions` and `/wrap-up` skills live only on `redesign/main`; changes to them target `redesign/main`.
- In a zsh script, `"$SHA:path"` applies a zsh modifier (`:a` makes an absolute path), so `git show "$SHA:apps/..."` reads the wrong object; write `"${SHA}:path"`.
- The automation tab can open inside the owner's own Chrome window (window 57, beside his pinned Gmail tabs), so `resize_window` resizes that window, and the tab is visible only while it is the window's active tab. To bring it forward: give the page a unique `document.title` prefix with the JavaScript tool, find that tab in `orca computer get-app-state --app com.google.Chrome --restore-window` output (the tab strip lists it as "<title>: parte do grupo ..."), click its element index, then confirm `visibilityState` visible. Restore the window to 1352 by 849 when a sweep ends.
- Google's account chooser and consent pages are driven with `orca computer` (the extension cannot act on `accounts.google.com`). Take a fresh screenshot immediately before every coordinate click there: the "Claude começou a depurar" banner appears and disappears and shifts every row by about 56 points, and a stale screenshot once picked the wrong account (cancelled at the consent page). Prefer element-index clicks on a link or button; a container row may not respond to an element click.
- `node tools/release-plan.mjs` once refused production with "GitHub comparison at repos/thomasluizon/orbit-ui-mobile/compare/478dbc1e...b84bdc9e diverged or omitted commits" and the same command succeeded a minute later with every baseline at the expected SHA; rerun it once before treating that refusal as real.
- The relay threshold is checked only at `Stop` until `#1126` lands, so an unattended turn that never ends can run far past it. Until then, measure the transcript with `readSessionContext` from `tools/lib/session-context.mjs` before starting long work in a turn, and end the turn when it is past the threshold so the hook can begin the relay. A width sweep with screenshots spends context quickly; measure before starting one.
- A gated launch script that is killed while holding its lock directory leaves the lock behind and blocks every later launch; the gate records its owner pid and reclaims a dead owner's lock. Never run a launcher inside a foreground command: a call that outlives its timeout moves to a 30-minute background task, which kills the worker at that limit.
- The local hermetic layout project can fail `press-shape.spec.ts` hover predicates at a different control each run when the one-minute load is far above 20 (several workers running); rerun it on the unfixed base before treating it as a regression, and prefer a quiet machine for merge checks.
- Pub/Sub delivery to the API is provable from Cloud Monitoring without any log line: `pubsub.googleapis.com/topic/send_request_count` on the topic and `pubsub.googleapis.com/subscription/push_request_count` (label `response_code` `ack_200`) on the push subscription, read through the Monitoring API with a `gcloud auth print-access-token` bearer piped into curl, never printed.

- Play reviews a new app only with its first closed, open or production release; an internal-only app keeps the "<package> (unreviewed)" name and "Enviar app para revisão" stays locked even with every setup task done.
- The Cloudflare token in the Keychain entry `orbit-cloudflare-api-token` is an Account API Token (it verifies at `/accounts/<id>/tokens/verify`, not `/user/tokens/verify`), on account `29945c90bc934c629c8e5a11cbfd146b`. The owner granted it Workers Admin at product scope, because Cloudflare's Workers roles let only Admin create a new Worker (Editor updates existing ones). It cannot read its own token record, and it cannot query Workers Observability through the API (`10000 Authentication error`), so read the pinger's invocations in the dashboard (Workers & Pages > orbit-staging-pinger > Observability). The account's workers.dev subdomain is `useorbit`, created through `PUT /accounts/{id}/workers/subdomain` because a cron trigger needs one (`10063`) and provider 5.26 has no resource for it; the pinger script's own workers.dev route stays disabled.
- claude-in-chrome may be connected to a remote Windows Chrome as well as the Mac one: run `list_connected_browsers` and `select_browser` the macOS one before any browser step. Play Console renders lists, wizards, downloads and dialogs only when the automation window is in front. `orca computer get-app-state --app com.google.Chrome --restore-window --no-screenshot --json` brings it in front (the page then reports `visibilityState` visible); `osascript` activation and `open -a` do not. Only the window's active tab becomes visible, so work in one tab and navigate it rather than opening a second tab.
- `tools/create-worktree.mjs` passes `--repo path:<p>` to Orca unchanged, so the path must be absolute; `path:.` fails with `repo_not_found`.
- Orca runs the repository setup script (`npm install`) in every worktree it creates; an `npm ci` started while it runs fails `ENOTEMPTY` with exit 190. Wait until no `npm install` or `npm ci` process has its working directory in the new worktree, then run `npm ci`.
- `node tools/list-bot-threads.mjs --re-review` refuses `--wait-seconds 0`. To request a fresh Pullfrog review without holding a waiter, post a plain `@pullfrog review` comment and read the verdict later with `--wait-seconds 0 --no-request`.
- Claude Code expands `@/path` mentions in a `-p` prompt into file attachments before inference, even with an empty tool list. Every headless `claude` call on untrusted text (a ticket body, a review dossier) runs with `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_ATTACHMENTS=1`.
- Play refuses a new default listing language until every subscription has a translation in that language; Orbit Staging's `orbit_pro` carries the en-US name "Orbit Pro", as production's does. The Orbit Staging listing requires screenshots on the 7" and 10" tablet slots too.
- expo-router resolves a bare `/` to the first group index statically, whatever `Stack.Protected` hides, and a replace to a guarded-out route is a silent no-op; navigate from an unguarded screen to an explicit destination.
- A local Expo release build reuses the Metro cache in the shared system temp directory, which can bake an older `EXPO_PUBLIC_*` value into the bundle; give each scratch build its own `TMPDIR`.
- A Play data safety change copies cleanly through "Exportar para .csv" on one app and "Importar de arquivo CSV" on another.
- The instrumented repro build (the device repro recipe above) needs, in its scratch worktree only: the shipped public Google client id (read it from the shipped bundle; a placeholder id sends Google to an error page with no OAuth state), a fresh `TMPDIR` for every rebuild (Metro reuses an older `EXPO_PUBLIC_*` value), `usesCleartextTraffic: true` under `expo-build-properties` in `app.json` (release builds refuse cleartext to the local mock at `http://10.0.2.2:5186`), and `ORBIT_APP_VARIANT=staging` for Orbit Staging. The mock answers `POST /api/auth/google/code` with a hermetic token and serves the hermetic fixtures.
- The Render MCP cannot query the production or staging Postgres: both IP allow lists hold only the operator Mac, so read-only measurement runs through local `psql` from that Mac.
- `ioreg -n Root -d1 -a` reports `CGSSessionScreenIsLocked`; while it is true, Chrome reports `outerWidth` 0 and Play Console renders only its account header, so console steps and width sweeps wait for an unlocked screen.
- Merging a harness change to `.claude/settings.json` and fast-forwarding the main checkout activates the new hooks in the running session.
- `tools/test-tools.mjs` run inside a worktree that is behind `origin/redesign/main` fails every ticket-tool case on the `.claude/orchestrator.json` staleness guard; read those as branch staleness, and judge the remaining cases. `tools/check-i18n-usage.mjs` resolves only literal translation keys, so an indexed key expression such as `map[operation].labelKey` is unresolved; use literal keys at the call site.
- `main`'s `tools/surface-manifest.mjs` has no `--check` flag and `main` has no `tools/check-i18n-usage.mjs`, no `tools/check-sonar-paths.mjs` and no hermetic layout project, so a combined merge check on `main` runs npm ci, the forced type check, the three Vitest suites and the web build.
- `tools/wait-ci.mjs --repo ui --pr <n> --require-check pullfrog-approval` keeps a registered wake source alive until Pullfrog publishes its approval status on a green head; a plain waiter exits as soon as the workflow checks settle, which leaves a sleep run with no wake source while it waits for a review.
- Two pull requests that each edit the `DESIGN.md` registry row of `CLAUDE.md` or the same drawing conflict with each other, so a combined merge check cannot merge both; merge one, then send the other a base-merge order.
- A read-only search subagent's verdict about a pull request branch is a lead: it once reported a branch's added rows missing because it read the main checkout. Confirm such a claim with `git show <branch>:<path>` or `git diff <base>...<branch>` before acting on it.
- Merging one approved pull request can turn a sibling that shares files with it into a conflict, which then needs another base merge and a fresh review. Before merging, run `git merge-tree --write-tree --name-only` of each remaining open head against the post-merge tree and merge first the pull request whose files the others build on.
- The surface manifest commits cells without `surfaceCount` or `cellCount`; summaries derive both totals from the cells. Pull requests that add different surfaces can merge without totals drift. `node tools/surface-manifest.mjs --check` still rejects missing, extra or changed surface records, naming each affected surface.
- A red proof swaps the pull request's locale files into the shared scratch base only while it holds the Playwright lock; a swap done before waiting for the lock is undone by the previous run's restore, and the spec then fails at load for the wrong reason.
- A layout spec copied onto an older base that imports messages the base lacks fails at load; prove it red with the pull request's own `en.json` and `pt-BR.json` swapped into the scratch copy, and tag new `data-slot` hooks into the unfixed DOM when the fix adds them, so the red comes from the defect.
- `merge-review-batch-body.mjs --ui-scope` replaces the body's `## Review harness` with the batch report's lines. When a batch on a UI pull request changed only tests, its report says "not applicable: test-only diff outside UI_SCOPE", and `tools/check-review-harness.mjs` rejects every not-applicable line except the motion one on a pull request with UI files; merge such a report without `--ui-scope` so the first sweep's lanes stay.
- Pullfrog can approve a head whose required checks are red (it approved `ui#1530` with "Upgrade layout geometry" failing 96 cases). Readiness reads every check at the exact head, never the approval alone.
- Next's app router registers its window `popstate` listener when the router mounts, before any page hook, and Chromium runs window listeners in registration order whatever the capture flag, so a hook cannot stop Next's history restore with `stopImmediatePropagation`. The Vitest history harness (`apps/web/__tests__/support/next-app-router-history.ts`) does not model that order, so a history bug can pass Vitest and fail in Chromium.
- The hermetic layout project runs with reduced motion, and the reduced-motion rule in `globals.css` turns every transition into a 0.01ms `all` transition, so a geometry read in the same frame as a focus or state change can see the start value. A spec waits for the element's animations (`element.getAnimations()` finished) before reading a box.
- A red-local proof of a spec that imports something the base lacks reports "No tests found", which proves nothing: swap the needed files into the scratch copy first.
- A small pill's `::after` hit extension (`.orbit-pill-action.touch-target::after`) is the `DESIGN.md` Buttons rule (the hit area grows to 48, the visible edges stay), so `expectInteractionFill` ("a hit extension must also carry the fill") applies to rows, tabs, chips and menu items, never to a small pill; a layout spec that walks an invitation's actions skips `.orbit-pill-action`.
- SonarCloud's "Coverage on New Code" combines line and condition coverage from the lcov the PR Tests run uploads as the `unit-test-coverage` artifact (`gh run download <run> -n unit-test-coverage`). Istanbul `DA` lines are statement starts, so a change inside a JSX attribute or a style object adds no coverable line; an uncovered new condition (`BRDA` with 0 hits on a changed line, such as a ternary) is what lowers the figure.
- On macOS the hermetic layout harness renders the sidebar shortcut hint as "Ctrl K" too, so a sidebar label case reproduces locally.
- A red proof counts only for a spec that is green on the pull request's head: read CI's layout job on the head before posting one, because a spec that cannot find its subject (a `::placeholder` is not a text node, so the label-fit contract counts 0 labels) fails on the base and the fix alike. Run a red proof with the one-minute load under 20; at 30 the hermetic cases time out at 60 seconds and prove nothing.
- An absolutely positioned pseudo-element that extends a hit area counts in its element's `scrollWidth`, so a layout guard that flags text overflow through `scrollWidth` reports every expanded control. Expand hit areas without layout padding (a wrapper's padding moves visible edges and widens action row gaps), and measure text overflow through the text extent.
- Playwright treats `launchOptions` as a worker-scoped fixture: a `test.use({ launchOptions })` inside a `test.describe` aborts collection of the whole file ("Cannot use({ launchOptions }) in a describe group"), so a spec that needs one sets it at file scope.
- Focusing the Hoje dock composer opens the conversation overlay and hides the pinned slot, so a layout spec that types into the dock with `fill()` measures the conversation composer instead (no Astra glyph, 48 wider) and loses the draft; open the conversation, type there, close it, then measure the dock.
- Web tags reach the habit form through a server action (`getTags`), so the Next server fetches `/api/tags` from the hermetic mock API and a browser `context.route` never applies; seed tags through the hermetic session the way calendars are seeded. Goals still fetch in the browser.
- A decision log entry takes its time from `date` at the moment of writing; an estimated time is wrong even when close.
- Mobile component tests render with `react-test-renderer` (every file under `apps/mobile/__tests__`). `@testing-library/react-native` is no longer declared; an order never asks a worker for RNTL.
- The hermetic layout harness runs desktop Chromium with the app's styled 4px `::-webkit-scrollbar`, a classic scrollbar that takes layout width, so `scrollbar-gutter: stable` on the shell scroller (`#1192`) reserves 4 on the trailing side. A layout spec measures content edges, widths and centres against the scroller's client box with the gutter read live (`apps/web/e2e/layout/scrollbar-geometry.ts` once `ui#1563` merges), never against `[data-shell-column]` or a hard-coded compact width.
- PR Tests (Unit Tests, then SonarCloud) takes 35 to 50 minutes, so a `wait-ci.mjs` waiter started at a push can end on its 45-minute `CEILING` before they finish: read the checks, then restart it.
- A CI layout red on a case the pull request does not touch is compared with the local full run of the same head before it counts: rerun the failed job once (`gh run rerun <id> --failed`) and file the race at its root.
- Linux headless Chromium (the CI renderer) rounds glyph advances to whole pixels and macOS does not, so a label with only a few pixels to spare fits in a local layout run and wraps in CI (the pt-BR Hoje empty-state title at 320 measured 218 wide on macOS and 228 on Linux in a 220 measure). A Linux-only layout red reproduces by replaying the rendered DOM in the `mcr.microsoft.com/playwright:<version>-noble` image through Docker Desktop.
- `orbit-api` `ErrorCopy` (`src/Orbit.Application/Common/ErrorCopy.cs`) and `LocalizedErrorResultFilter` exist only on `orbit-api` `redesign/main`, so API work that localizes errors targets `redesign/main` until the redesign merge.
- `tools/wait-ci.mjs` lists the names of checks from cancelled duplicate runs as `failingChecks` in its settled line; read `gh pr checks <n>` at the head before treating any of them as red.
- On a direct load of `/habits/new`, Next writes its own history state (`__NA` and `__PRIVATE_NEXTJS_INTERNALS_TREE`) before the habit creation guard's `replaceState` and `pushState`, at 1x and 6x CPU throttling, so the guard's entries always carry Next's keys. The empty description field is already in the server HTML, so a layout spec that presses browser Back on a creation form first waits for the armed guard (`history.state.orbitHabitCreateSentinel === true`); Back before that leaves the document.
- `main` has neither `tools/check-dependency-edits.mjs` nor `.github/scripts/check-critical-production-cves.mjs` (both exist only on `redesign/main`); `main`'s `Dependency Audit` runs `npm audit --omit=dev --audit-level=critical`. A `main` ticket names `main`'s own checks.
- `launch-worker.mjs` admission counts every open pull request in the three repositories plus live reservations against `maxOpenPullRequests`; a finished launcher's reservation is held 5 minutes after release (`RELEASE_HOLD_MS`), so a relaunch inside that window is refused with exit 8.
- A `claude-in-chrome` session has no tab group at start, and creating one opens a new window, which the owner forbids; the owner's Chrome is a 1352 wide window he uses. A rendered sweep therefore needs another method: an `orca computer` tab in the open window with DevTools docked (device toolbar for 600, 840 and 1100 widths, console for the probes), done only while the owner is not using that window.
- A CI-only layout race often reproduces locally under CDP CPU throttling (`Emulation.setCPUThrottlingRate`, rate 6) in a scratch copy of the spec; a history-write probe through `page.addInitScript` that wraps `pushState` and `replaceState` shows the real write order in Chromium, which the Vitest history harness does not model.

- A Chromium page built with `setContent` and no viewport meta, in a context with `isMobile: true`, lays out at 980 and scales the visual viewport down; Playwright then reads a `screenshot` `clip` in visual-viewport pixels, so a clip taken from `getBoundingClientRect` captures the wrong region. A test page that emulates a phone carries the app's `width=device-width, initial-scale=1` meta.
- Creating a worktree through `tools/create-worktree.mjs` starts Orca's own `npm install` in it; an `npm ci` started at the same time fails with `ENOTEMPTY`. Wait for those installs to end (`pgrep -f "npm install"`), then run `npm ci`.
- A Linux replay of a Chromium test runs in Docker on the `mcr.microsoft.com/playwright` image with its bundled Chromium (Google Chrome stable does not install on Linux arm64); it reproduced a Linux-only raster red exactly.

Local Orbit Staging APKs build in about four minutes from any commit (`build-1658.sh` in the scratchpad: the staging `google-services.json` comes from the Firebase Management API for app `1:627063127862:android:bf6b662eeaf713cea77d97`, never printed; `set-app-version.js` stamps a version at or above the staging floor). UI `main` is `93ea7f10f`, API `main` is `2b44af74`, landing `main` is `4cded614`; staging web serves `93ea7f10f`, production web `7f51c19ab`; staging API serves `2b44af74`, production API `cd9f6401`; staging and production landing serve `4cded614`.

### Resume state and source ownership

This is an automatic relay with the chain open: the successor adopts the run through `adoptRelayRun` and keeps its state. Read the carried decision log (D1 to D14 of the current chain, then the earlier log through D163) before launching. The full live inventory is `handoff-inventory-current.json` in the durable scratchpad, with explicit per-branch and per-worktree dispositions. `handoff-pr-heads-current.json`, `wrap-up-progress-live-receipt.json` and `wrap-up-questions-dispositions.md` retain the live source and reporting checks. Current UI product base is `0d4ebb92d28332145b20384b79f57159d358101b`; API base is `8ac0be8d24398be490a5fb1aa2101dd5d354cb65`. Derive the current documentation head from `origin/main`; this handoff itself changes no runtime source.

The owner restored the user-level Codex default to `gpt-6.1-sol` at `xhigh`, and a default-model probe answered on it. Start the fresh coordinator on that default. This does not alter Astra's application model or repository worker profiles, and does not guarantee future provider availability. All three workers completed successfully during the coordinator interruption, so inspect their results rather than relaunching them.

`tools/start-relay-successor.mjs` spawns a `claude` successor, which suits a Claude coordinator. A Codex coordinator must first implement or use a verified Codex-only relay, because the transcript metrics reader returns no measured Codex calls or context maximum. Owner-origin `--close-chain` is a metadata-only path and launches no engine.

### Astra acceptance and diagnostic limits

The current staging discriminator prepared a complete held preview for 174 synthetic targets, then typed post-tool continuation budget denial returned HTTP400 and lost the result. Unique pending membership, audit, response hash and source establish that new boundary; temporal Render correlation alone does not establish the original unavailable historical cause. The diagnostic unknown-outcome flag stays immutable. No retry, confirmation, consumption, argument revision, quota reset, model override or account creation was performed. There are 171 exact owned QA fixtures plus three protected pre-existing habits; cleanup remains a separately admitted root action after fresh pending-disposition checks. An expired auth cache needs the ordinary protected refresh, never a fabricated login or copied secret.

API1612's local handler tests are real source composition with internal deterministic inference, not paid staging acceptance or a fabricated OpenAI transport response. After review and carrying deployment, root must prove real budget-admitted requests, truthful held versus saved JSON/stream outcomes, explicit approval and independent persisted readback before closing. The all-Astra real-call and rendered sweep still follows all current Astra ticket completion and staging deployment. `#26` lacks admitted QA/grader/live eval evidence; do not turn skipped, unavailable or invented tool traces into passes.

For `#418`, the successful CI mutation jobs tested zero mutants because explicit configuration allowlists omit the changed correction/preparation owners. Retain thresholds and use the installed supported changed-owner selection with frozen since-target `2c4381c66d4c502d7f47af29e3a909350882441c`; do not call selector syntax or the no-mutant run a proof. Keep the unrelated-name valid-candidate control and two-fence forged-identity calibration distinct.

## Batch history

The text of `## The order` before the board triage, kept verbatim as context: closed work, owner rationale and round history. It is never placement; `## The order` above is the only placement.

Reconcile the open board with these batches before each handoff. Place each new ticket exactly once. A batch completes before the next begins. Within a batch, use dependency order; drive already open pull requests first.

#### Batch M: move to the new stack, before everything

The decision and its research: brain ADR `Move Orbit to Render with Amazon SES, a free staging and manual release workflows.md`. File one ticket per coherent piece (`repo:api`, `repo:ui` or `repo:landing`, `needs:no-conversation`) and place every one here. The batch ends when staging and production both run on the new stack, every item below is verified live, and the retired plans are cancelled.

1. Tooling: DONE. Terraform, the AWS CLI, the Render CLI, libpq (`psql`, `pg_dump`) and the Render MCP (user scope, next session) are installed and proven by read calls; AWS, Cloudflare, Render, PostHog, Sentry and Supabase all answer.
2. Infrastructure as code in `orbit-api` `infra/`: the official Render Terraform provider (`render-oss/render`) for the production and staging services, Postgres and environment groups; the AWS provider for SES; the Cloudflare provider for DNS (move `useorbit.org` DNS from Spaceship to Cloudflare, which Turnstile needs anyway). Terraform state never enters a repository (they are public): an S3 backend with native locking in the AWS account, and a local state file outside every repository until that account exists (`terraform init -migrate-state` moves it). Import the existing production API service (`srv-d6tc2isr85hc739bf75g`, Ohio) so its URL does not change.
3. Production: API web service, Next.js web service (standalone Docker image built in GitHub Actions, pushed to GHCR, deployed by digest), landing as a Render static site, Render Postgres 17 in the same region. Size from measured use (the API averaged 0.004 CPU and 343 MB over 30 days): API and web at 0.5 CPU / 512 MB, Postgres 1 GB; raise the API to 1 CPU / 2 GB when memory nears its limit.
4. Staging: web on the free plan; API on the free plan with a scheduled `/health` ping every 5 minutes only between 08:00 and 24:00 America/Sao_Paulo (the 750 free instance hours a month are shared by every free service); Postgres on the free plan, recreated and re-seeded by a scheduled workflow every 4 weeks (free databases expire 30 days after creation). Staging deploys only through `release.yml` with a selected branch, never automatically.
5. Data: copy the production database from Supabase to Render Postgres (schema and data, including `hangfire`) in a short maintenance window, verify row counts table by table, switch the API connection string, and verify the live app end to end.
6. Google sign-in in the API, web and Android, including the calendar scopes, replacing Supabase Auth. Keep the Supabase Auth path working for installed Android builds until `AppConfig.MinSupportedVersion` passes the first build with the new sign-in (expand, then contract). Add the new redirect URIs to the existing Google OAuth client.
7. Email on Amazon SES: verified domain with DKIM through Terraform, production access, bounce and complaint handling, and the waitlist contacts moved from Resend into Orbit's own database.
8. Release workflows: one manually run `release.yml` per repository with an environment input (`production` always `main`; `staging` any selected branch): `orbit-api` builds, runs the EF Core migration bundle, deploys, checks health and records a GitHub Deployment; `orbit-ui-mobile` builds and deploys the web image; `orbit-landing-page` deploys the landing. `android-release.yml` keeps a track input with only open, production (both `main`, production API) and internal (a selected branch, staging API). The current `deploy-api.yml`, `deploy-web.yml`, `web-image.yml` and `deploy-landing.yml` are folded into these and deleted. Render and Vercel auto-deploy are off.
9. The `/release` skill in `orbit-ui-mobile`: for production, compare each service's last production deployment with `main` (API, web, landing, Android), deploy only the services with undeployed changes, in that order, wait for each and verify it; for staging, take a branch and deploy it to the staging services the same way.
10. Cutover and retirement: move `app.useorbit.org` and `useorbit.org` to Render, then cancel Vercel Pro and remove its domains; cancel Resend Pro after SES is live; pause the Supabase project after its Auth path is retired, keeping a final database dump. Once the owner signs off on the verified migration, delete the retired projects and cancel every retired subscription so nothing stale stays active; a permanent deletion that only a browser can do is the owner's click. Record each step in the brain.

Owner rules for Batch M: the production copy loses zero rows (writes frozen during the dump, every table verified by row count and checksum, Supabase paused until sign-off); staging seeds sample data for the owner's account only; PostHog replaces Vercel Analytics and Speed Insights; both databases stay reachable through the Render MCP and `psql`; MCPs, CLIs and APIs come before the browser.

Tickets, in dependency order (every other Batch M ticket is closed):

- DONE. Email runs on Amazon SES only, Resend is retired and its account deleted, and the paused Supabase project and the two Vercel Orbit projects are deleted.

Open pull requests first, in this order (heads are leads; re-read each). Each merges on the bar; a pull request behind its base merges after a local merge-result check, or at its approved head when the base’s newer commits share none of its files (D115), and every merge of a pull request behind its base also passes `npx turbo run type-check --force` on the combined merge first (a file-disjoint base can still break a type through a changed component contract). A base merge or a body edit needs a fresh Pullfrog approval of the new head. A pull request whose `DESIGN.md` change also edits the `DESIGN.md` row of the `CLAUDE.md` docs registry conflicts with every sibling that does the same: send a mechanical base-merge order that keeps both additions in the one row.

The document, image and voice buttons leave the bar; one "+" button opens a menu with the photo, document and other attachment options. Today it is a tall multi-line box with three icons inside and the glyph and send outside.
- Tab bar: more vertical padding; the icons sit too close to its top edge. The Progresso icon reads as a mountain; use a dashboard-like glyph.
- Notifications: no bell anywhere in the mobile shell, so a person cannot reach Avisos. The Avisos drawing places the bell in the mobile shell header and in the web sidebar.
- Calendário header: the month and the year controls need padded hit and hover areas; the small superscript year looks wrong; the Mês, Semana, Período, Agenda selector is not centred.
- Calendário: the "Mostrar hábitos que se repetem" switch is out of place on the screen (move it into a menu or settings sheet); the status legend wraps to two lines and looks poor.
- The floating back-to-top button shows on Calendário (and overlaps the stat tiles); it belongs only on Hoje.
- Calendário day card: too cramped, too many strings, too little spacing. Habit names wrap ("Ler 10 / minutos") beside "21:00 · sem registro"; the event row truncates its title; the stacked "Importar 1 evento" and "Calendários" pills (`#1123`); the Google Calendar sync card (connected, never synced, auto-sync switch, Sincronizar) does not belong inside the day card.
- Calendário stat tiles: captions wrap or truncate ("Melhor sequên…", "Registro / s", "Sem registr…").
- Perfil: the top group rows need leading icons (the Astra glyph for Astra, a bell for Notificações, and so on), like the Mais do Orbit rows.

How this work runs: first a multi-agent research Workflow (the owner asked for one, which is the explicit opt-in) that starts from the brain (the `Orbit debloat and redesign master plan` note holding the whole redesign conversation, the design direction ADR, the redesign ADRs and the ui-skills shortlist note, read through the Obsidian MCP) and then covers UI and UX best practice with mobile design philosophy first (native Android and iOS patterns, progressive disclosure, sheets and menus over crowded screens, spacing, touch targets, label length), with Codex used for research too through a sanctioned path (the guardrail refuses a raw `codex exec`; add a research-only launcher path if none exists). Then one `/questions` round to the owner on what the research changes, even in a sleep run (his instruction for this round only). Then correct `DESIGN.md` and the affected drawings, then a full sweep of every screen and every component on both platforms, fixing everything, then rendered sweeps until a pass finds nothing.

The research is done: the Workflow synthesis (`0 Inbox/raw/orbit-mobile-review/research-final.json` in the brain vault), the Codex arm, and their reconciliation, all in the note `2 Areas/20-29 Orbit Engineering/Research - native mobile feel for the Orbit redesign.md` (section "Codex research arm and reconciliation"). The owner's `/questions` round is answered (brain ADR `Native mobile review answers on suggestion chips, the Avisos bell and long typed text.md`; the rules are in the standing rule on native mobile feel above). Every ticket below carries a binding "Owner answers and research reconciliation" section; a worker reads it before the JSON's wording.

The mobile review tickets, all `repo:ui` except `#1136`, every one fixed on Android and web:

- Done: `#1136` (`orbit-api#693`, merged to `redesign/main`, released to staging): a proactive check-in body is at most 60 text elements, and an over-cap body becomes a complete localized fallback ("Você ainda tem N hábitos pendentes hoje."), never a cut sentence. `#1135` (`ui#1513`, merged): the whole Hoje proactive line is one press target. `#1133` (`ui#1511`, merged): the native mobile rule, the large-text rule and the amended drawings, including the stat compositions `#1144` implements, are in `DESIGN.md`, the canvas and the contracts. `#1145` (`ui#1514`, merged): Perfil top group rows carry leading icons. `#1137` (`ui#1515`, merged): the Hoje, habit detail and conversation composers are one pill with the glyph, the field, a "+" attach menu and send inside; Hoje suggestion chips show in the conversation. `#1139` (`ui#1512`, merged): tab bar M3 padding, padded tab fills, the dashboard Progresso glyph, keyboard-safe pinned actions and the onboarding scroller clearance. `#1142` (`ui#1516`, merged): the Calendário header, options menu with the checkable repeat filter, and the legend sheet. `#1140` (`ui#1517`, merged): the Hoje bell row, the web sidebar bell and the scrolling bell rows on Progresso and Perfil. `#1138` (`ui#1519`, merged): the measured chip peek, chips that wrap at large text, chip labels without habit titles. `#1154` (`orbit-api#694`, merged to `redesign/main`, released to the staging API): every retrospective habit stat carries an optional habit id. `#1144` (`ui#1524`, merged): Calendário figures carry the drawn captions ("Best", "Logged", "Not logged"; "Recorde", "Registros", "Sem registro"), Progresso shows three figures with the short weekday and the top habit as a list row, the legend opens in a sheet; "Active days" and "Top habit" depart from the drawn "Logs" and "Habit" because they describe their values. The whole first wave is merged and closed: `#1148` (`ui#1522`, Calendário week grid and agenda rows named "{title}, {time}"), `#1147` (`ui#1523`, Hoje rows on one content edge, the parent progress label under the title above font scale 1.3), `#1152` (`ui#1525`, loading stat placeholders inside compact tiles at large text), `#1143` (`ui#1521`, the Calendário day card with the title on its own line and the sync controls in the Calendários sheet), `#1155` (`ui#1526`, the Progresso top habit row opens its habit by the response id; the habit detail header draws a controls row (well, rename, log ring) above a full-width title that wraps, and the Habit Detail drawing is amended to match). - The owner's staging report (Novo hábito showed "Foram muitas tentativas. Espere um instante.", Hoje fell to "Ocorreu um erro"), first in this batch: `#1308` a read that meets a 429 waits for `Retry-After` and retries instead of failing (the shared query rule stops retrying a 429 only on `redesign/main`; the events ticket route turns an upstream 429 into a 500; the stream's first failure invalidates every account query), so it lands on `redesign/main`. The 429's source is found and filed as `#1309` (blocked by `#1308`): Render answers a request from another Render service to a sleeping free service at once with `429 Too Many Requests` (plain text, no `Retry-After`) and does not wake it, so the staging web BFF can never wake the staging API; only an outside caller (the browser, the Android app, the Cloudflare pinger, which runs 08:00 to 23:55 Sao Paulo) does. A probe through the BFF got that 429 on 18 of 18 calls over 90 seconds while the API stayed asleep; a call from outside woke it in 52.6 seconds and the BFF then reached it. `#1309` maps that 429 to a 503 with a new code in the BFF and wakes the API from the browser with one no-cors `/health` fetch while `#1308`'s retry waits; no paid plan (D143 keeps staging free).

the Wrapped back control lives in its own in-flow row above the cover (absolute positioning to dodge layout is a banned structural hack); focusing the shell composer opens the conversation as `Composer.d.ts` draws, so layout guards follow focus into the conversation; Markdown headings are 28, 22 and 17 on both platforms; light-mode hover on opaque controls gets its own value that clears 1.25:1; a drawn string beats a ticket paraphrase and a worker's second-opinion dissent (the orchestrator frames `/second-opinion` as a claimed defect, so DISAGREE approves); the pt-BR slipping line keeps the drawn "Nenhum registro", matching the app's pt-BR "Registros" for completions; the rescue card, slipping line and proposal request show only for a good habit (the API never marks a bad habit overdue); the pt-BR delete verb is "Apagar" everywhere; an existing type token may replace a drawn size that cannot fit the longest locale value (`--fs-lg` for the best-day tile); the Perfil "Análise de uso" switch stays; the Perfil persistent reminder row and its feature are deleted; a flexible habit is done for the day once logged; a toast host follows the topmost open modal; the sign-in column is centred vertically at phone width and the Entrar and Verificacao drawings are amended to match; a control with an opaque resting fill layers `--bg-hover` over that fill on hover (no new token); the MCP pending Google sign-in store is capped at 1,000 with eviction on insert; every sheet footer uses the drawn trailing pair (ghost Cancel, then the filled action, hugging their labels at the trailing edge); the focus-ring layout guards count a ring drawn by a `::before` or `::after` pseudo-element and still flag a wrapper-plus-pseudo double ring. Android wide sheets stay on TrueSheet, width-capped to the centred shell column with `anchor: center` and bottom-attached (TrueSheet ignores `presentation` on Android and `detached` is web-only), recorded in DESIGN.md as a platform-adapter difference; a drawn off-scale value (the Hoje Astra action 3px underline offset and 6px margin) snaps to the spacing scale because `## Bans` outranks the drawing, and the underline is the platform text underline; the solid badge fill is the neutral well, as the Pro and Assinatura drawings say; skip and postpone act at once with an Undo toast backed by the API undo (DESIGN.md: a reversible action without a working undo keeps its confirmation); the web version shown in Sobre and Suporte is the served commit, because web has no release version; `.dockerignore` keeps `apps/web/e2e` and the Playwright config out of the web image, so a test-only import in an e2e spec cannot break the image build; date-only values keep their calendar day in every time zone; every pt-BR plural message renders zero in the plural; a return after the stale time keeps the post-open stream refresh and drops the focus refetch for the account families the stream owns (`#987`); TimeField's bounded hour, minute and day-period columns are the one recorded nested-scroll exception, on the conditions in `DESIGN.md` `### Scroll ownership` (`#1046`); a TalkBack or other device-only acceptance check is carried to the owner's redesign gate review as a manual step, never a merge gate (`#1068`); two pull requests that edit the same control merge in the order of the ticket that owns that control's behaviour. Hoje row metadata stays `fg-3` at rest (the design system's `--text-meta`) and takes `fg-2` only under its nested hover or press fill; a contrast fix to a selected RadioRow covers every text that row paints (description, meta, disabled reason); a habit creation exit the person approved replaces the discarded creation history entry rather than leaving it behind the destination.
